# دستور الثوابت المعمارية وسجل التدقيق (Architecture Invariants & Audit Ledger)

### 2026-10-25 P0-A updated invariants and test evidence

- **TRANSFER-PACKAGE-COMPLETE:** Never delete/import target tenant rows if archive inventory omitted a live table (including FK-owned child tables), contains undeclared/malformed tables or imported row count differs from declared row count. Abort the transaction. Older incompatible archives must be converted/tested safely, never accepted by disabling checks.
- **RESTORE-SNAPSHOT-ATOMIC:** A restore snapshot record is part of the same transaction as destructive tenant data replacement, not a post-commit statement that can fail after successful restoration. Session/cache invalidation is post-commit.
- **CLOUD-BACKUP-DISABLED:** `enabled=false` blocks cloud backup uploads, regardless of actor privilege. Distinct `canManageBackups` access remains mandatory.
- **VERIFICATION-SCOPE:** Isolated GitHub Actions run `40188575959` passed targeted P0-A regressions, backend typecheck/build/guards, PostgreSQL fresh migration history and migration-255 physical catalog checks plus core integrity; Electron source updater contracts passed. Separate new restore-snapshot change `d5fc804...` awaits linked CI `40188927253`. Historic `NOT VALID` rows, legacy archive rollback, concurrency, cross-module service traces and actual packaged Electron remain unverified. See `docs/reviews/PLATFORM_CORE_SECURITY_P0A_20261025.md`.


### Platform Core P0-A — ثوابت جديدة 25 أكتوبر 2026 (غير مختبرة تشغيلًا)

- **AUTH-PASSWORD-VERSION:** MFA challenge and newly issued session are bound to the password hash verified during authentication. Admin resets invalidate in-flight logins; concurrent failure counters acquire a tenant-scoped row lock. A concurrent MFA setup must not overwrite confirmed enrolment.
- **AUTH-CLOUD-SESSION-HEADER:** A Cloud SaaS `x-session-id` header never bypasses cookie/CSRF session authentication, even if `ALLOW_SESSION_ID_HEADER` is set. Local desktop header compatibility is retained.
- **TENANT-JOIN-AND-FK:** Financial, reporting, partner and maritime joins bind *both* `tenant_id` and entity ID. New migration 255 defines NOT VALID tenant-composite FKs for MFA and maritime records, preserving historical violations for audit without validation/mutation.
- **BACKUP-NONDESTRUCTIVE-PREFLIGHT:** Destructive backup restore requires a matching tenant identity and refuses a missing table with live tenant data or unsupported child rows. Restore fails on sequence errors. Older incompatible ZIP files are explicitly not guaranteed restorable; safe migration path remains outstanding.
- **MFA-ENCRYPTION-BOUNDARY:** Globally known desktop encryption fallback is only permitted in explicit local runtime mode; a stray Electron/Portable flag on Cloud cannot enable it.
- **AI-SECRETS-MANAGEMENT:** AI key configuration endpoints and service writes require settings management rights, never authentication alone.
- **PAYMOB-HMAC-INGRESS:** Paymob callback query HMAC is routed to the cryptographic verifier; controller never itself treats the presence of a signature as proof of payment.
- **EVIDENCE:** [P0-A source audit follow-up](docs/reviews/PLATFORM_CORE_SECURITY_P0A_20261025.md), regressions authored only, zero tests/build/typecheck/guards/migrations executed this turn. Full Platform Core is NOT release-certified.


- **BILLING-SANDBOX-CLOUD-DENY:** محاكاة دفع اشتراك تُنشئ اشتراكًا حقيقيًا، لذلك لا يمكن الوصول إليها من Cloud SaaS مهما كانت مفاتيح البوابات؛ خدمة الإيداع المباشر تحرس نفسها قبل DB. فشل بوابة حقيقية لا يتحول إلى فاتورة دفع افتراضية.
- **BACKUP-PLATFORM-IMMUTABILITY:** النسخة التي يتحكم بها Tenant لا تنقل ولا تعيد صفوف `PLATFORM_TENANT_TABLES` (بما فيها الاشتراكات، المدفوعات، الجلسات وOffline ID leases). المنع في الاستكشاف، التطبيع، والحذف/الإدراج، مع قبول أرشيفات قديمة دون تطبيق بيانات المنصة منها.
- **BACKUP-CONSISTENT-SNAPSHOT:** كل جداول النسخة تُقرأ من معاملة `REPEATABLE READ` واحدة. لا تُبتلع أخطاء الحذف أثناء Restore عندما تكون قيود FK معطلة مؤقتًا.
- **AUTH-COOKIE-TRUST:** `Host` / `X-Forwarded-Host` لا يلغيان `Secure` في Cloud؛ استثناء localhost محلي فقط. `PermissionsGuard` لا يمنح إذنًا دون AuthContext ولا يرفع دور `super_admin` خارج نطاق المنصة.
- **SESSION-ATOMIC-CREATION:** يعاد التحقق من حالة المستخدم وهو مقفول داخل المعاملة، ويُحدث المستخدم وتُنشأ جلسته بصورة ذرية. يجب تطابق `account_id` و`tenant_id` للجلسة والمستخدم.
- **RESTORE-SESSION-REVOCATION:** نجاح استرجاع النسخة يستلزم إبطال AuthCache وكاش إعدادات وفروع وباقات المنشأة، وإلغاء جلسات الشركة داخل معاملة الاسترجاع أو نقلها، بلا تجاهل لأخطاء DELETE.
- **AUTH-RATE-MODE-BOUNDARY:** `POST /api/auth/login/mfa` محروس بعدّاد محاولات الدخول؛ لا يجوز لأعلام `IS_ELECTRON` و`PORTABLE_MODE` أن تلغي التحديد في Cloud أو في وضع تشغيل غير صالح.
- **DESKTOP-BACKUP-MODE-BOUNDARY:** جدولة النسخ ومسارات حفظ الملفات المحلية تتطلب وضع تشغيل محلي صريح ومعترف به عبر `isDesktopRuntimeMode`، لا الاعتماد على علم Desktop وحده.
- **حالة التحقق:** تغييرات مصدرية + اختبارات انحدار مكتوبة **لم تُشغّل**. لم تُشغّل Tests أو Build أو Typecheck أو Migrations أو اختبار قاعدة بيانات، ولم يحدث Deploy/دمج. [نطاق الأدلة والجزاءات غير المفحوصة](docs/reviews/PLATFORM_CORE_SECURITY_P0A_20261025.md).

### Core Architecture Audit 01 — إصلاحات 23 أكتوبر 2026 (بانتظار التحقق التنفيذي)

- `config/app-mode.ts:isDesktopRuntimeMode` يحسم صلاحيات Desktop من وضع محلي صريح ومدعوم؛ لا تمنح `PORTABLE_MODE` أو `IS_ELECTRON` صلاحيات محلية فوق Cloud أو وضع غير معروف. `validateEnv` يرفض تعارض Cloud مع العلمين قبل الاتصال بقاعدة البيانات. فحوص المطور والتحديث والنقل والإقلاع وحدود الهجرات 74/78/79 تستخدم هذا المصدر؛ لم يتغير SQL الخاص بالهجرات ولا تُعاد الهجرات المنفذة.
- `tenant-boundary.ts:getPlatformTenantIds` مصدر مشترك للحُرّاس وتنقية الأدوار في bootstrap. يُحمى `zs` دائماً مع هوية المنصة المضبوطة للتوافق مع التثبيتات القديمة؛ `default` و`dev-tenant` هويات محلية فقط. لا يجوز للتنقية أو إعادة تسمية المستخدمين إسقاط هوية تعترف بها الحُرّاس.
- وصفات Compose السحابية والإنتاجية تثبت وضع Cloud، وتمرر نطاق المنصة وإعدادات SMTP. يجب توفير CORS وCSRF صراحة؛ فحص الجاهزية يستخدم `/api/health/ready`، وقاعدة API للواجهة فارغة لأن مسارات العميل تحتوي `/api` بالفعل.
- رفض `/api/auth/me` الصريح بـ401/403 يسبق fallback الهوية المحفوظة، حتى لو فشل طلب حالة التفعيل المستقل. فشل الشبكة وحده لا يلغي دعم الأوفلاين أو كتالوج POS.
- في المراجعة النهائية: عند نجاح طلب حالة التفعيل تظل بوابتا التفعيل والإعداد الأولي سابقتين لرفض جلسة غير موجودة بعد؛ لا يُرسل تثبيت Electron الجديد إلى login قبل إتمامهما. تنقية أدوار المنصة وإعادة تسمية المستخدمين تطبّع `tenant_id` بنفس قص المسافات وتحويل الحروف الذي تستخدمه الحُرّاس.
- **استثناء صريح من المالك في هذه الجلسة:** تبقى كلمة مرور المطور الثابتة وقبولها في Desktop كما هما؛ ARCH01-01 مستثناة من الإصلاح. لا يسمح هذا الاستثناء بفتح اللوحة في Cloud.
- أضيفت حالات انحدار للمصادقة وعزل البيئات وحماية `zs`. لم تُشغّل Tests أو Build أو Typecheck أو migrations ولم يحدث نشر؛ الإصلاحات منفذة في المصدر وتنتظر التحقق المأذون به. لم تبدأ مراجعة الحزمة 02.

> **الغرض:** هذا الملف ليس حصراً للميزات (ذلك في `SYSTEM_CAPABILITIES.md`). هذا الملف يجيب على أسئلة مختلفة:
> **ما الذي يجب ألا يُكتب أبداً؟ وأين المصدر الوحيد للحقيقة؟ وما الثوابت المفروضة؟ وماذا دُقِّق وماذا لم يُدقَّق بعد؟**
>
> **لمن يقرأ هذا الملف بعد انقطاع (بشر أو مساعد ذكي):** ابدأ من القسم 1 ثم القسم 2. القسم 2 وحده يمنع أكثر من نصف الأخطاء التي رصدناها فعلياً في هذا المشروع.
>
> **قاعدة إلزامية:** أي إصلاح لثغرة بنيوية، أو ثابت جديد يُفرض، أو نمط يُحظر — يُوثَّق هنا فوراً.

### HR stabilization checkpoint — 24 October 2026

- HR-REVIEW-2026-10-24: Parallel main commit 572a122 restores an Egyptian EOS formula attributed to Article 125. This article concerns **annual leave scheduling and settlement**, not a universal EOS gratuity schedule (Egyptian Law 14/2025). That change is deliberately NOT incorporated; the dedicated Egyptian statutory EOS path remains blocked until verified. The safe main commit 8efd025 (portal ISO timestamps) was incorporated separately.

- HR-SEC-1: Leave listing must filter tenant_id at root query and tenant-scope joins.
- HR-PAY-1: Attendance, leave, statutory and loan deductions are accounted for ONCE. Never materialize the same automatic tax/attendance deduction as employee adjustment and built-in payroll deduction.
- HR-PAY-2: Monthly wages and fixed compensation are prorated to the hire date, valid contract dates and payroll period. Only active contracts apply. Approved and paid payroll are immutable.
- HR-ATS-1: Marking a candidate as hired requires one atomic transaction containing employee, contact, active salary contract, applicant status and vacancy headcount; generic stage editing may not bypass hiring. Stage updates acquire the same row lock and salary-bearing hire requires hrSalaryManage permission.
- HR-EOS-1: Persist only applied/capped loan and asset recoveries. Draft settlement must not terminate an employee, and posting/termination must be atomic. Unpaid wage must be manually reconciled and confirmed.
- HR-EOS-3: Final posting re-locks live employee loan balances and rejects stale draft deductions before recording the GL journal; full repayment allocation must reconcile in the same transaction. Approved-but-undisbursed loans are not recoverable by EOS.
- HR-EOS-4: Settlement absorption rejects negative/nonfinite entitlements and deductions; end-of-service preview validates exact termination dates and positive salary, keeping precision in service years. Regression cases authored but not executed.
- HR-EOS-5: Financial EOS preview/list requires salary view/manage or payroll approval privilege; creating/deleting settlements requires hrSalaryManage and posting requires hrPayrollApprove, not broad `hr`. All settlement list/detail joins include tenant_id on employee, department and journal.
- HR-EOS-7: EOS frontend hides create/delete actions without hrSalaryManage and accounting posting without hrPayrollApprove; server remains the permission enforcement boundary.
- HR-EOS-6: Custody in statuses 'lost' or 'damaged' is not silently treated as returned during settlement preview or posting; a documented clearance/recovery decision is required under the existing gate.
- HR-EOS-2: Do not silently choose Saudi jurisdiction or apply an invented Egyptian universal gratuity formula. Egyptian statutory EOS requires a legally verified module; automated Egyptian calculation and the older employee-profile preview are currently blocked. Direct manual termination requires loan/custody clearance, and both status and contract updates are transactional.
- HR-WPS-1: Never export bank payroll with fake identity/account details or before approval. Generic SCR/EDR SIF is not a country-certified WPS file; only review CSV is currently available.
- HR-LEAVE-1: Leave requests must be tenant-bound, date-valid and non-overlapping under employee-row lock. Display employee annual leave from the same annual_leave_balance/used_annual_leaves counter maintained by HR approval/cancellation; never count all leave types as annual.
- HR-PORTAL-1: Portal requests lock the employee row exactly as HR admin does before overlap checks; employee payslips are visible only after payroll run and item approval, never in drafts/review; all joins are tenant scoped. A true zero annual leave balance must display zero, not a 21-day fallback.
- HR-PAY-3: Recalculate manual payroll adjustments using the original full payroll engine. Rebuilding from base salary alone loses overtime/commissions and can alter loan and statutory deductions.
- HR-PAY-6: Overtime uses the payroll policy multiplier, not a hardcoded 1.5. Hourly staff whose punch wage already covers ordinary worked minutes receive only the extra `(multiplier - 1)` premium. Exact local statutory multipliers still require country and case validation.
- HR-PAY-7: Rebuilt draft/reviewed payroll runs automatically exclude stale employees who are no longer eligible, without deleting manual adjustment history. Attendance, exceptions, and leave deduction day ranges are clipped to the employee's effective hire/termination dates; otherwise a partial-month salary can be over-deducted.
- HR-PAY-8: Hourly punches are clipped to actual hire, contract and EOS service dates before generating base pay. Attendance absence and lateness exceptions occurring during approved leave cannot generate a second overlapping deduction; unpaid leave is handled by its separate payroll deduction.
- HR-SEC-2: Daily attendance joins must scope all employee, department, job title and punch tables by tenant_id. Review-only bank CSV text must escape formulas, quotes and control characters.
- HR-EMP-1: Primary phone and initial salary contract are created atomically with the employee; salary contract currency comes from validated tenant settings, never a hardcoded EGP default. Civil/national IDs must not be hardcoded to the Egyptian 14-digit length across GCC tenants.
- HR-EMP-3: All salary contract CREATE/UPDATE paths derive currency from validated tenant settings and refuse mismatches. New contract numbering follows canonical CON-YYMMDD-XXXX format, not global sequence-only CON-n.
- HR-PAY-4: Employees terminated during a payroll interval remain payable for earned days on active/ended historical contracts, except where a posted EOS settlement already reconciled final wages. Salary periods are clipped by hire, contract and employment termination dates.
- HR-EMP-2: An employee termination cannot be accomplished by generic employee update, status patch or deactivation; those paths preserve terminated records. Initial and amended annual leave entitlements must actually persist to the employee row.
- HR-PAY-5: Payroll approval validates the effective-dated accountant-reviewed country configuration; missing/unreviewed statutory rates fail closed, and policy edits after payroll review require renewed review. Reviewed runs can be re-reviewed on the same row lock; stored corrupt policies error instead of silently disabling deductions. Approved/paid payslip reads are immutable and never dynamically recomputed against current laws. Policy editing and payroll approval use a shared tenant-specific advisory transaction lock and update is audited; statutory-eligible employees cannot be paid with those policies disabled. This is a **configurable calculation**, not proof of certified legal compliance. Official national tax/insurance verification remains a release blocker.
- HR-LEAVE-2: Employee self-service and admin leave creation must acquire the same employee-row lock and reject overlapping pending/approved leave spans; statutory leave days are not inferred from client-supplied counts. Recruitment contracts use canonical daily document numbering. Employee status UPDATE predicates cannot race into reactivating terminated staff.
- HR-EMP-3: Employee profile partial updates must preserve absent user, identity, organization, payroll, insurance, tax, and bank fields instead of silently nulling/resetting them. Explicit null/blank values can intentionally clear nullable fields.
- HR-STAT-1: All hardcoded 2024 tax bands and implicit Egyptian 11/18.75 insurance rates removed from payroll calculations, INCLUDING the exported generic legacy tax helper (it now requires explicit progressive brackets). Enabling statutory deductions requires explicitly entered, reviewed, effective-dated per-country rates/brackets, and rejects stale/missing configuration. Tax brackets use annual upper bounds and percentage rates. Legal verification of each country, residency eligibility, and annual tax settlement are still external release blockers.
- HR-PAY-10: Approval re-executes the production payroll line engine under the reviewed run row lock and rejects any changed financial snapshot (wages, adjustments, statutory deductions, loans, included employees); an exception rolls back the recalculation and forces an explicit new review.
- HR-SEC-3: Payroll materialization updates explicitly tenant-scope every changed payroll item, employee adjustment and loan allocation; global numeric primary keys are not permission boundaries.
- HR-PAY-11: Salary changes across effective contracts require calendar-day segmented wage proration, with explicit failure on contract overlaps or gaps. The chosen contract displayed is the latest segment, not the only salary source; multi-month fixed allowances must scale by actual calendar-month fractions.
- HR-PAY-15: Payroll refuses contract currency mismatches with tenant currency, and temporarily fails closed for 3-decimal KWD/OMR until HR payroll and general-ledger numeric storage and bank exports support correct currency precision end-to-end. Two-decimal rounding must never be asserted as KWD/OMR correctness.
- HR-PAY-14: Approved overtime on days with different salary contracts uses each day's effective monthly wage converted to its daily/hourly rate, never the blended monthly rate. Overtime with no unique active contract is rejected.
- HR-PAY-12: Effective-dated fixed monthly compensation packages are prorated over their own validity intervals; overlapping packages cannot silently double-pay or override one another. Gaps in optional compensation pay zero, unlike gaps in salary contracts.
- HR-PAY-13: When the explicitly reviewed policy prorates insured salary for partial-month employment, both the statutory wage MINIMUM and MAXIMUM caps must be prorated by the same employed calendar-month fraction.
- HR-PAY-9: Canonical HR line calculation must aggregate contractual and manual earnings before computing statutory deductions; country-reviewed configuration must explicitly choose insurable wage source, handling of partial-month employment, taxable earning base and insurance deduction from tax. Review-stage attendance calculations are NOT payroll tax sources. Runtime payroll rebuild invokes this canonical engine for the actual stored payroll item and rejects invalid amounts; approval and journal posting consume these persisted totals. Numeric regression fixtures are synthetic policy examples, not legal rates.
- HR-STAT-2: Monthly tax AND social insurance withholding are **only** applied to complete calendar-month periods. Other payroll frequencies require verified period-specific conversion rules; otherwise calculations fail closed to prevent applying an entire monthly insurance floor to one weekly run. Payroll policy tests are authored but not executed.
- Open release blockers: Egypt/Saudi/Kuwait/Qatar/Oman effective-dated labor, tax, social insurance, EOS and WPS rules; leave accrual/rotas; bank acceptance; payroll numerical and cross-tenant HTTP tests; UAT.
- This change set has NOT been typechecked, built, or test-executed: production readiness cannot be asserted.

Reference commits: a4ac7bf, 0fcac5c, fc0b54c, f0a9186.


### HR payroll integrity follow-up — 24 October 2026 (unexecuted regression gates)

- HR-PAY-16: Cross-frequency runs ('all' versus weekly/monthly, or employees moved between pay frequencies) cannot include overlapping payable days for the same employee. The creation path uses a tenant-wide payroll transaction lock, while the approval path checks overlapping employee line membership under the same lock. A cross-frequency run cannot reuse an existing run ID even when the date range is exactly identical.
- HR-PAY-17: Scheduled withholding aggregates due installments against each individual loan's actual remaining principal; accrual-side payroll withholding never exceeds debt. Approval allocation uses the SAME period-end due-date boundary as payroll calculation, preventing premature recovery of later installments.
- HR-PAY-18: Payroll accrual rejects a persisted gross/net/deduction mismatch in integer cents, and confirms that stored operating deductions cover employee insurance plus income tax before GL posting; a balancing plug is prohibited.
- HR-PAY-19: Payroll item inclusion/exclusion and manual allowance/deduction creation/removal acquire the payroll-run lock and revalidate draft status inside the transaction, preventing edits crossing the reviewed/approved boundary.
- HR-PAY-20: SQL row-lock syntax is `LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE` (never `FOR UPDATE LIMIT 1`). Fixes the payroll-run status locking query shared by recalculation, review, approval and payment.
- HR-PAY-21: Cancelling only a draft/reviewed run must release its own applied employee adjustments atomically; otherwise canceled deductions/bonuses are never picked up by later payroll. Row-lock status checks prevent cancellation after approval.
- HR-PAY-22: Approval blocks unresolved hourly punches and requires an accountant-reviewed effective-dated jurisdiction even when deductions are configured as disabled. All payroll accruals reconcile each individual persisted employee payslip, not just aggregate totals.
- Verification: numeric regression specs are authored but NOT executed; build/typecheck/guards likewise NOT run. Currency precision for KWD/OMR and complete nationality-specific legislation remain blocked. No main merge or deployment.

- HR-PAY-23 (24 October follow-up): All draft/review/run creation and manual-line mutation paths use the same tenant payroll advisory transaction lock before the run lock, to serialize applied adjustments across overlapping run candidates. Policy country must match the tenant currency before approval; no statutory rates are derived from that mapping.
- HR-PAY-24: The explicit `earned_base` insurance wage source includes contractual earnings, allowances, commissions and overtime minus unpaid attendance/leave, rather than ignoring everything but base salary. Non-finite or negative manual adjustments are rejected. Date-only and payroll month input use calendar validation.
- Awaiting: end-to-end tenant/currency and legal-country acceptance tests, official country-dependent residency/nationality coverage and statutory settlement/WPS certification. KWD/OMR still intentionally blocked pending coordinated 3-decimal migration.
- Numeric tests authored but NOT executed; no build, typecheck or guards invoked.

- HR-PAY-25 (24 October follow-up): Salary-contract and fixed-compensation mutations acquire the tenant payroll lock and reject edits overlapping any approved/paid employee payslip (both original and proposed validity intervals). Future salary/allowance changes require new effective-dated entries; booked historic payroll must use independent adjustments.
- HR-WPS-2: Bank review CSV cannot default to EGP when tenant currency is missing and cannot export 2-decimal KWD/OMR data. Audit events clearly label it an un-certified REVIEW CSV rather than falsely claiming an official WPS/SIF export.

### HR full-stabilization continuation — 25 October 2026 (unexecuted test gate)

This checkpoint records only **code-proven** adjustments on `fix/hr-stabilization-multicountry-20261024`. It does **not** certify the HR module, bank exports, legal country rules, or deployment.

No tests/build/guards executed.

Approved/paid historical entries remain immutable and require supervised review; migration has NOT been executed.

Asset writers now serialize against payroll/EOS approval, verify employee tenant membership and active service, refuse reassigning historical/closed custody, reject unrepresentable cents, and prohibit changing an asset status when its linked recovery adjustment already exists. Changing accrued recoveries requires an independent, explicit reversal or adjustment workflow; not a silent status flip. Reconciliation/migration of preexisting rows using noncanonical `amount_type='amount'` remains a DATA FIX and must be audited before payroll processing. No tests/build/typecheck/guards run.

### Accounting ledger hardening (October 2026)

ARCH02 local implementation and unexecuted acceptance work are tracked in [the ARCH02 closure register](docs/audits/ARCH02_FIX_STATUS_2026-10-24.md). Its implementation status is not evidence that tests, migrations, builds, or environment checks passed.

- Use exclusive UTC next-day bounds for report/closing cutoffs. Block sign-off on unresolved postings, missing sales journals and due financing accruals.
- Keep operational partner/inventory openings out of the aggregate cash/bank opening journal. Partner creation, opening balance, subledger and GL share one transaction.
- Domain postings own their shared subledger synchronization. Never use shipping-line/subcontractor IDs as supplier IDs; retain explicit tenant-scoped mappings. Manual control-account entries and aggregate control-account FX revaluation cannot bypass that ownership.
- Cheque transitions use type/state validation, stable sequence-based event identities, immutable base-currency snapshots and actual action dates. Set reversal links only when all accounting dimensions exactly offset; incurred bank fees remain incurred.
- Value a receipt from the stock ledger's reconciled `globalAfter - receiptQty`, under product locks. Keep unit costs at six decimal places and document money at currency precision. Manufacturing consumes actual cost snapshots and posts on completion, with conversion costs separate from materials.
- Defer financing income and recognize it through the effective-interest schedule. Settings currency and base-rate metadata must agree; base currency cannot be reinterpreted after rates/books are initialized. Historical ambiguity requires reviewed reconciliation, not inferred monetary evidence.
- Non-cash journals do not generate cash flow. Show ambiguous mixed cash flows as unclassified until attribution is available. Preserve explicit manual cash-flow classification when reversing its journal.

- Keep the original and opposite journal entries `posted` after reversal so their effects offset in every posted-only report. Older cancelled originals with posted reversals require a separate tenant-scoped historical reconciliation before repair.
- Serialize tenant journal postings, drafts, lock changes, and month/year transitions with the same transaction advisory lock. Year-end closing and reopening entries can bypass an existing month lock only after validating the fiscal year state and end date.
- Allocate final journal numbers from the inserted row ID under the tenant unique index; use a UUID for the temporary number. Recheck and lock manually selected accounts inside the posting transaction so deactivation cannot race the posting.
- Recheck drafts while holding that lock; exclude closing and reopening entries from operating profit. Validate tenant ownership of journal and treasury references before writing.
- Cap depreciation at cost less salvage and the cumulative amount earned by the posting month. Require actual foreign currency balances for monetary account revaluation and recompute under the posting lock; a single spot rate cannot reconstruct foreign units from the local ledger.

### Purchase and inventory integrity (October 2026)

- Purchase orders must validate supplier, warehouse and product IDs against the same tenant before create or draft update. A tenant-scoped header does not make foreign references safe.
- GRN invoices and direct purchase invoices lock the supplier row before checking normalized vendor invoice numbers, so concurrent creation cannot pay the same supplier document twice.
- A location adjustment derives its branch from the tenant-scoped location. A branch-only adjustment is rejected because the shared stock ledger would otherwise change unassigned stock while tagging the movement to that branch.
- Product master data cannot overwrite the WAC of stock on hand. Positive opening stock posts an inventory debit and opening capital credit in the same transaction as the product and stock movement; old unposted opening balances require tenant-scoped reconciliation.
- Landed costs apply only to posted purchases and post on their recognition date through the central period-checked journal. Split inventory and COGS by replaying classified global stock movements after the receipt through the weighted-average pool. Unclassified or missing movement history blocks automatic allocation rather than guessing.
- INV-TRANSFER-262 (28 October 2026): Migration `2040000000262_fix_stock_transfer_item_variance_constraint.ts` updates check constraint `chk_transfer_item_variance` to permit `((received_qty = 0 AND variance_qty = 0) OR variance_qty = dispatched_qty - received_qty)`. In final issue transfers (`issueMode === 'final_issue'`), `received_qty` is populated with `item.qty` and `variance_qty = 0`, ensuring operational issue orders post cleanly without check constraint violation.
- INV-IMPORT-0703659 (29 October 2026): Commit `07036590` — import lot allocation never silently skips. `allocateSaleFromImportLots` returns explicit per-item coverage (requested / allocated / shortfall / reason) instead of `void`; a preorder-to-sale conversion tops up uncovered quantity at the shipment lot's landed cost, else current WAC, through the pure `calculateImportSaleCost` engine — a partial allocation total is never used as the whole cost. A stock-reducing sale never completes without its COGS/inventory journal pair: the journal gate is decoupled from the revenue total (it posts when revenue OR cost is positive), and when no cost is knowable at all the conversion fails closed with `IMPORT_SALE_ZERO_COST` and the preorder stays un-invoiced. The fail-closed form is forced by a deeper invariant: `insertPostedJournal` rejects 0/0 journal lines, so a zero-cost COGS pair cannot be posted literally — a genuine zero-cost stock policy needs an explicit, recorded override, never a silent omission. POS lot shortfalls are traceability-only (POS GL COGS comes from WAC) and are out of scope of this invariant.
- INV-GRNI-MFG-P1 (29 October 2026): No hard-coded 2-digit money math in settlement, billing, verification or manufacturing paths. GRNI accrual, bill portions, GRN journals, GRN bills, three-way-match reconciliation and manufacturing cost accumulation all round to the tenant's book precision (`currencyMoneyDigits(tenantBaseCurrency)` — 2 or 3), never `* 100` / `toFixed(2)` / bare `toMoney()`. Storage must cover book precision or the code fix is silently defeated at insert time: migration `2040000000263` widens `purchase_items.grni_amount`, `manufacturing_work_orders.total_cost` and `manufacturing_wo_consumptions.line_total` to scale 3 (widening is value-preserving; rollback never narrows posted balances). The three-way-match discrepancy tolerance is one minor unit, not a fixed 0.01. Quantities keep their own precision (3dp) and stock-movement informational totals are not book amounts — only amounts that reach journals, bills or stored settlement balances are governed by this invariant.
- INV-PDC-RECYCLE-2D963DD (29 October 2026): PDC journals are unique per ACTION OCCURRENCE (cheque, posting_sequence, event) via dedup_key (uq_arch02_journal_event), NOT per (cheque, event type). Migration 2040000000252 narrowed idx_journal_entries_pdc_and_wht_uniq to once-per-cheque events (receive, collect, endorse, issue, clear) plus withholding_tax_remittance, deliberately EXCLUDING deposit, bounce and payable_bounce: a cheque legitimately re-cycles through deposit -> bounce -> restore -> re-deposit -> bounce, and each occurrence posts its own journal. Never re-widen that index to the 0247 definition: it turned re-deposit/re-bounce into 23505 soft skips (status advancing with no journal, subledger syncing without a GL leg). A restore after an under-collection bounce is INTENTIONALLY unlinked (reversal_of_entry_id IS NULL): its journal unwinds receive + bounce across 1122/AR, not the 1121 deposit leg, so it is not the exact opposite of the bounce journal. Never falsify a reversal link merely to free an index slot; fix the key granularity instead. Must-post PDC actions (deposit, collect, clear, bounce, endorse) fail the whole transaction when they produce no journal id; cancel / return / restore keep their conditional posting by design.
- INV-PDC-PRECISION (29 October 2026): PDC book amounts are computed in the tenant's minor units (pdcBookAmount takes required MoneyDigits; collect/clear settlement and FX difference and the bounce fee use toBookMoney). The cheque FACE amount is validated in the CHEQUE's own currency at creation (a USD cheque stays 2dp for a KWD tenant) and the bounce fee is validated strictly, never rounded. Storage: migration 0264 widens accounting_cheques.amount and bounced_fee to scale 3 (base_amount and settlement_base_amount were already widened by 0256). Never reintroduce a hard-coded *100 anywhere in the PDC path: the snapshot computes correctly, and any truncation between snapshot and journal cannot be recovered by insertPostedJournal normalisation.
- INV-ALLOC-PRECISION (29 October 2026): payment allocation is computed in the tenant's minor units end to end: validation accumulates minor units (no cents), remainders and unallocated amounts use roundMoney, and a positive remainder of any size is a real open amount (the old > 0.009 filters hid up to 9 fils per invoice and stranded them permanently; the old 0.005 -> 0.01 rounding admitted a 0.01 allocation against a 0.005 remainder). Storage: migration 0264 widens payment_allocations.allocated_amount and purchases.paid_amount to scale 3 (sales.paid_amount was already widened by 0258).
- INV-BANKREC-PRECISION (29 October 2026): a statement line matches a GL line only when the amounts are exactly equal at book precision (bankStatementAmountsEqual rounds both sides first, then compares). No tolerance-based auto-matching: the old < 0.01 tolerance matched KWD differences of up to 9 fils as "exact" and marked them reconciled, hiding real gaps. The workspace difference is rounded to book precision and isBalanced is true only at exactly zero; a genuine difference (e.g. a bank fee) stays visible until it is matched exactly or journalled -- reconcileMatch still posts no adjusting journal, and adding one is a feature, not a tolerance fix. Storage: migration 0264 widens bank_statements balances and bank_statement_lines.amount to scale 3.
- INV-DRAWER-PRECISION (29 October 2026): shift variance is computed at the tenant minor unit (computeCashDrawerVariance takes required MoneyDigits), any non-zero book variance requires a close note and posts to the GL (the old 0.01 gates dropped sub-cent variances from the GL while the shift stored them). Storage: migration 0264 widens cashier_shifts opening/expected/counted/variance to scale 3. KNOWN RESIDUAL, do not claim otherwise: the expected-cash COMPUTATION (computeShiftExpectedCashFromShift and its helpers) still rounds components with the drawer service's private toMoney (2dp), and blind-close declared totals are still 2dp-rounded; threading digits through those paths is the next drawer sub-item.
- INV-DRAWER-EXPECTED-CASH (29 October 2026): expected cash for a shift is computed at the tenant's minor unit end to end: computeShiftExpectedCashFromShift and all eight of its component helpers (drawer movements, sales breakdown, service breakdown, sale return totals, supplier payments, expenses, customer collections, cancelled sale receipts) round through toBookMoney(value, digits), where digits is derived from the tenant inside the aggregator when the caller does not pass it -- never hard-coded to 2. The legacy private toMoney (2dp) remains ONLY for blind-close declared totals, which are deliberately untouched (changing declared-total rounding changes the blind-close review comparison and needs its own verification). No migration was needed: the shift money columns were already widened to scale 3 by 0264; this entry closes the residual recorded in INV-DRAWER-PRECISION, which stays as history.
- INV-SUPPLIER-SCHEDULE-PRECISION (29 October 2026): Supplier payment schedule creation, installment calculation, and settlement payments are parameterised with the tenant's book precision (MoneyDigits). Installment count division computes equal base installments rounded down to the currency minor unit (or the explicit user roundingStep when provided), with the final installment absorbing the residual at book precision so that the sum of installments strictly equals the total without generating or dropping fractional minor units (fils/piastres). Purchase remaining payable accumulates in currency minor units (factor 10 ** digits) rather than hardcoded cents (* 100). Installment payments strictly validate precision against toMinorUnits(amount, digits), rejecting sub-minor and foreign decimal amounts with INVALID_AMOUNT. Settlement journals post at full book precision via addBookLine with bookDigits. In payment allocation, validatePaymentAllocationRequest enforces a fail-closed contract by requiring explicit MoneyDigits without default fallback.
- INV-NEGATIVE-STOCK-WAC (29 October 2026): one weighted-average cost policy for every receipt path, including after overselling. weightedReceiptCost accepts a negative beforeQty: sales and consumptions expense at the then-current cost_price, so the algebraic blend (beforeQty * beforeCost + receivedValue) / (beforeQty + receivedQty) preserves book value exactly through negative pools while its result stays non-negative. Two degenerate cases fall back instead of ever storing a negative unit cost: a receipt that exactly empties the pool (|afterQty| < 0.00001) keeps beforeCost, and an algebraic result below zero falls back to the incoming unit cost on a recovered pool and to beforeCost while still short. purchases-write (create, edit-apply, receipt) and goods-receipt use the engine; valueStockReceipt already did, so its 8 callers (maintenance x2, manufacturing, returns x2, sales auto-production, trade-in) are unblocked with no call-site change. No migration: products.cost_price is already NUMERIC with 6dp rounding in the engine.
- INV-SALE-EDIT-FROZEN-COST (29 October 2026): a sale edit never re-costs a physically unchanged line. updateSale deletes and rebuilds sale_items, and resolveSaleModifierCosts re-costs each rebuilt line at the current product WAC — so updateSale now restores the original per-unit cost_price for every rebuilt line whose physical composition matches exactly one old line, before the new rows are inserted and before postSaleEdit reads them. A line is unchanged when its product, its sold unit (unitMultiplier) and its modifier composition (sorted componentId|optionId|qty|consumptionQty per modifier) all match; quantity, selling price, discount and tax are deliberately not part of the key, so a quantity-only change re-prices the line total through qty * frozen unit cost without re-costing the unit. Each matching modifier's own costPrice is restored too, so the stored modifiers JSON stays consistent with the frozen line cost. A new product, a changed unit, a changed modifier composition, an ambiguous duplicate match, or a non-finite/negative old cost all keep the freshly resolved cost (fail-safe re-cost, never an invented cost). postSaleEdit needs no change: it already posts COGS from the rebuilt sale_items.cost_price. This is exact because updateSale permits only one edit per sale (SALE_EDIT_ALREADY_APPLIED) and blocks edits with existing returns, so the frozen cost is always the original sale's cost, not an intermediate edit's.
- INV-PURCHASE-RETURN-ORIGINAL-COST (29 October 2026): a purchase return is costed at the original purchase line's net cost, never at the current product WAC. createPurchaseReturn derives return_items.cost_price from purchase_items.unit_cost — after invoice discount allocation, per sold unit — stripped of VAT exactly as at purchase time (divide by 1 + taxRate/100 only when the purchase prices_include_tax), and the purchase_return stock movement uses the same cost per base unit (divided by unit_multiplier). postPurchaseReturn needs no change: it already journals from return_items.cost_price, so its inventory credit and its 5190 variance now reflect the original cost. A missing, negative or non-finite line cost, a non-positive multiplier, or a negative tax rate throws PURCHASE_RETURN_COST_INVALID instead of falling back to the current WAC.
- INV-POSTING-BOOK-PRECISION (29 October 2026): every posting method in the accounting posting service journals at tenant book digits, never at a hard-coded 2. The thirteen methods that still rounded through the private toMoney default and through addLine's default digits — payroll payment, payroll item payment, employee loan disbursement, employee loan manual repayment, delivery-rep settlement, van trip settlement, van stock transfer, van field collection, van field return, end-of-service settlement, customer opening balance, supplier opening balance, customer installment interest, plus installment interest recognition — now compute bookDigits from the tenant base currency (currencyMoneyDigits over tenantBaseCurrency) and use toBookMoney for every amount and addBookLine (addLine with bookDigits) for every journal line, following the shape the fortified methods already use. The two hard-coded 0.01 tolerances inside this set move to one minor unit (maxAllowedDifference(bookDigits)): the delivery-rep fee reconciliation and the van-trip shortage/overage thresholds, so a 0.005 KWD overage is journalled instead of ignored. After this package, a balanced-parse check over accounting-posting.service.ts finds no bare this.toMoney( call left.
- INV-WAC-QUANTITY-SOURCE (30 October 2026): there is exactly one WAC quantity source. applyStockDelta's globalAfter — reconciled from the sum of location balances — is authoritative; its globalBefore is only the cached products.stock_qty and can lag the location sum. Every WAC blend therefore recovers the true prior quantity as globalAfter minus the received quantity, via recoverWacBeforeQty in the inventory-valuation engine (3dp, the ledger's quantity precision): valueStockReceipt (returns, manufacturing, maintenance, sales auto-production, trade-in), the three purchases-write sites (create, edit-apply, receipt) and goods receipt. The recovery is valid only because none of these call sites passes skipGlobalUpdate to applyStockDelta (then globalAfter would just be the cached counter again); a future skipGlobalUpdate caller must not use this helper.
- INV-IMPORT-LOT-SHORTFALL-RECORD (30 October 2026): an import-lot allocation shortfall is never silently discarded. The preorder conversion already cross-checks coverage and tops up shortfalls (P0); ordinary POS sales now capture allocateSaleFromImportLots' result and record anomalous shortfalls as pos_audit_events rows (event_type import_lot_shortfall, amount = shortfall quantity, reason and metadata carrying requested/allocated/shortfall/reason) inside the sale transaction. Anomalous means: missing_location or invalid_quantity always, or no_active_lots / lots_exhausted for a product that has had import lots. A shortfall for a product that has never had an import lot is ordinary local stock, not a traceability break, and is not recorded. The sale is not blocked: for POS sales the GL COGS comes from WAC, so a lot shortfall is traceability-only.
- INV-LANDED-COGS-ACCOUNT (30 October 2026): there is exactly one COGS account for landed costs, and it is the tenant's configured one. applyPurchaseLandedCosts posts the sold portion of a receipt's landed cost to settings.cogs_account_id (5100 by foundation) whenever that account exists and is active, falling back to creating 5100 — never to a hard-coded 5110. The old code created 5110 on demand with the same name as 5100 but a different group (expenses vs cogs), so the same cost of goods sold was split across two accounts and any report summing 5100 alone understated COGS (and overstated gross margin) by the landed sold portion. A tenant that deliberately configured a different COGS account keeps it; the configured account wins.
- INV-INSTALLMENT-INTEREST-PRECISION (30 October 2026): allocateInstallmentInterest runs entirely in the tenant's minor units, passed as a required parameter — never a hard-coded 2. The allocation rows in accounting_installment_interest therefore sum to exactly the interest the journal leg posts at book precision, and recognition can never over- or under-release the 2155 deferred account. The old cents-based engine rounded a KWD 7.125 interest to 7.13 for the allocation rows while the journal leg was exact at 7.125, leaking 0.005 per plan into a permanently over-released deferred balance. The effective-interest shape is unchanged (the last installment absorbs the remainder; earlier installments earn more as the balance falls), and 2-decimal behaviour is unchanged.
- INV-HR-PAYROLL-BOOK-PRECISION (30 October 2026): a payroll run computes, stores and posts entirely at the tenant's book precision, passed as a required minorUnits parameter through the payroll-run, payroll-calculation, statutory and hourly-attendance engines and the loan-allocation engine -- never a hard-coded 2. Migration 2040000000265 widens every column on the input -> run-item -> loan-allocation chain to scale 3 (run items, adjustments, loan deduction allocations, contracts, compensation packages, hourly rate, insurance salary, employee adjustments, loans, installments and the employee ledger), so a KWD salary of 100.125 survives storage and sums exactly (gross 120.13, net 106.779 in the pinned example); requireStoredHrMoney and requireLoanMoney still throw on any fraction finer than book precision instead of silently rounding, requirePayrollMonetaryCurrency no longer blocks KWD/OMR runs, the journal-reconciliation tolerance is one minor unit, and the WPS review CSV formats at book precision. The old chain rounded every component to cents, threw on a third decimal at save time, and rejected KWD/OMR payroll runs outright. 2-decimal behaviour is unchanged. Deliberately out of scope: EOS settlements (engine and storage, next sub-package), attendance-penalty previews, employee-withdrawal-balance previews (pinned at 2), leave-day balances, and recruitment expected salary.
- INV-EOS-BOOK-PRECISION (30 October 2026): an end-of-service settlement computes, stores and posts entirely at the tenant's book precision, passed as a required minorUnits parameter through the EOS engine -- never a hard-coded 2. Migration 2040000000266 widens all eleven money columns of hr_end_of_service_settlements to scale 3, so a KWD gratuity of 50.063 survives storage exactly as the book-precision journal posts it; the service validates every manually entered settlement amount at book precision (requireStoredHrMoney) instead of rounding it to cents, formats preview amounts at book precision, and reconciles EOS loan allocations with half-a-minor-unit tolerances instead of hard-coded 0.005. The old engine rounded every component to cents and the old storage rounded 50.063 back to 50.06 at insert time. 2-decimal behaviour is unchanged. Deliberately out of scope: attendance-penalty previews, employee-withdrawal-balance previews (pinned at 2), leave-day balances, and recruitment expected salary remain separate packages; the EOS service's housing_allowance / transport_allowance / gross_salary contract columns do not exist in the schema, so totalSalary falls back to base_salary plus zeros -- a pre-existing phantom-column defect, not a precision issue, and not changed here.
- INV-INTEGRITY-CHECKS-EXPANSION (30 October 2026): the accounting integrity registry audits at the tenant's book precision and sees every one-journal source. The four ledger/subledger drift checks use half a minor unit derived from the tenant's settings currency (0.005 for 2-decimal tenants, 0.0005 for 3-decimal tenants; an unreadable currency keeps the old 0.005 rather than blinding the report), DUPLICATE_SOURCE_POSTINGS counts only active journals (status posted, reversed_by_entry_id IS NULL) across all dedup_key, partial-unique-index and SELECT-guard source types, and three PDC_CHEQUE_* checks pin the per-cheque chain (one active journal per cheque+event, the cheque's latest-journal pointer is a live PDC journal for that cheque, no orphaned PDC journal). SALE_EDIT_NET is unchanged (sale + sale_edit share a source_id space). Deliberately unchanged: the INVENTORY_GL_VALUATION 0.05 aggregate slack, pending an explicit decision on whether it is WAC rounding headroom or a tolerance to make currency-aware.
- INV-RACE-WINDOW-JOURNAL-UNIQUENESS (30 October 2026): a partial unique index on journal_entries (tenant_id, source_type, source_id) WHERE reversed_by_entry_id IS NULL (migration 2040000000267, idx_journal_entries_race_window_uniq) is the race-proof guard for the eight one-journal source types whose post methods only SELECT before inserting (manufacturing_work_order, delivery_rep_settlement, van_field_return, hr_payroll_item_payment, food_waste, sale_edit, customer_opening_balance, supplier_opening_balance); the partial predicate keeps postSaleEdit's legitimate reverse-then-repost legal, the post methods catch 23505 on this index and return the existing journal (except postSaleEdit, which fails closed so a concurrent edit is never reported as this edit's posting), and the migration refuses to run while legacy active duplicates exist (run the 7a DUPLICATE_SOURCE_POSTINGS check on production data first).
- INV-RETRY-HANDLER-COVERAGE (30 October 2026): the accounting recovery worker replays every failure row whose post method takes (queryable, sourceId, auth) and is idempotent on replay (24 distinct source types: the original 11 plus food_waste, hr_payroll_accrual, hr_employee_loan_disbursement, hr_employee_loan_repayment, manufacturing_work_order, supplier_payment_schedule_settlement, trade_in, customer_installment_interest, hr_end_of_service_settlement, and the four argument-free PDC cheque events); methods needing a payload the failure row does not store (amount, payment channel, revision, return items) and stateful replays (sale_edit) are deliberately excluded and keep failing loudly. Before calling a handler the worker rechecks for an ACTIVE journal (posted, not reversed) in the same transaction and resolves the row when one exists, because a 23505 inside that transaction aborts it and a handler's own catch-and-look-up-existing cannot run there; no recordPostingFailure call site is added inside a business transaction, since the rollback would erase the record — the failures table is fed by manual/ops inserts, the 7d remediation discovery, and future deferred-posting paths, never by a doomed transaction.
- INV-SALE-EDIT-REMEDIATION-WIRING (30 October 2026): the sale-edit remediation engines are runnable only through SaleEditRemediationService and its two accounting-controller endpoints: discovery is always a dry run that writes nothing, and apply posts reversal journals only for an explicit, reviewed saleIds list plus a literal confirm flag, re-discovering immediately before repairing so a sale that changed since the report is never repaired blindly; each sale commits in its own transaction, the engines never call postSaleEdit for backfill, and there is no scheduled or automatic apply anywhere.
- INV-GATEWAY-CURRENCY-AMOUNTS (30 October 2026): every storefront gateway amount and currency goes through the gateway-amount engine, and the currency is always the tenant's own base currency at that currency's ISO 4217 exponent — never a substituted currency and never a hard-coded x100. Stripe unit_amount and every webhook amount check convert with toGatewayMinorAmount (exponent 0 for Stripe's zero-decimal currencies, 3 for KWD/BHD/OMR and their peers via currencyMoneyDigits, 2 otherwise), Tap charge amounts round with toGatewayMajorAmount at the same exponent, and resolveGatewayCurrency returns the tenant base currency for Tap/Stripe (EGP only for Paymob/XPay by integration contract; an unset base keeps the historical provider default, SAR for Tap and USD otherwise — the defect was overriding a configured currency, not the default for an unconfigured one). The old code charged a KWD 10.123 Stripe order as 1,012 fils (a 10x undercharge that passed the webhook check because the check used the same x100), truncated every Tap charge to 2 decimals (a silent 0.003 shortfall per KWD order), and forced SAR for Tap on EGP/unset-base tenants (an EGP total charged as SAR, ~13x, passing the webhook check by construction). Deliberately unchanged: Paymob/XPay EGP cents math (correct at exponent 2), the Tap hashstring signature scheme (its 2dp formatting is Tap's own construction, not an amount check), the Tap re-fetch identity comparison, and mock/sandbox session paths.
- INV-PAYROLL-ACCRUAL-BOOK-PRECISION (30 October 2026): the payroll accrual allocator runs entirely in the tenant's minor units, passed as a required minorUnits parameter — never a hard-coded 100. allocatePayrollAccrual converts every total with the shared toMinorUnits/fromMinorUnits helpers (the payroll-loan-allocation idiom: explicit fail-closed validation for non-finite, negative, or finer-than-book inputs) and postPayrollAccrual passes its bookDigits and builds the journal with addBookLine, so a KWD accrual posts exactly (gross 10.008 = net 5.002 + loan 2.002 + asset recovery 1.002 + deductions 2.002; salary expense 10.125 stays 10.125, not 10.130). This closes a gap in INV-HR-PAYROLL-BOOK-PRECISION, whose "entirely at book precision, never a hard-coded 2" claim did not hold for this engine: the old cents allocator rejected book-exact 3dp totals whose cents roundings did not reconcile, and when the cents check passed it overstated the salary-expense debit by up to half a minor unit while bare addLine calls re-rounded every other line to cents. 2-decimal behaviour is unchanged. Deliberately unchanged: the accrual journal's entry date (today, not the wage period — the P1-3 period-cutoff gap, a separate package), the SELECT-guard/23505 idempotency floor, and reconcilePayrollRunJournalTotals, which remains an uncalled parallel reconciler (its divergence from this allocator is a separate item, not wired in here).
- INV-WAGE-PAYOUT-PAYROLL-JOURNAL (30 October 2026): a quick-cash wage payout settles the approved payroll run item through the payroll payment journal — never through the loan path. createQuickCashAdvance splits by actionType: the advance branch is unchanged (loan row, one installment, Dr 1160 / Cr 1110 loan-disbursement journal, loan cap check, draft-run rebuild), while the wage_payout branch creates NO loan row and NO installments, skips the loan cap check and the rebuild, and instead resolves the latest approved run item (run approved/partially_paid, item approved) BEFORE any cash moves, requires the amount to equal that item's net pay at the tenant's book digits (HR_WAGE_PAYOUT_AMOUNT_MISMATCH) and an approved item to exist at all (HR_WAGE_PAYOUT_NO_APPROVED_ITEM), posts the per-item payroll payment journal through the existing postPayrollItemPayment primitive (Dr 2140 payroll payable / Cr 1110 cash from the drawer, source hr_payroll_item_payment keyed by item id, with the 7b unique-index backstop), marks the item paid WITH its journal link (paid_at/paid_by/payment_channel/shift_id/journal_entry_id, mirroring payPayrollRunItem), and records the employee-ledger row against the run item (entry wage_payout, balance_after 0). The old code ran both branches through the loan path and marked the item paid with no amount check: 2140 was never relieved, the item could never be paid again through the normal path (whose guard reads status approved), the books showed an advance receivable for earned wages, and the loan installment would later deduct the same wages from a future run. The amount gate at the top of the function now runs at book precision (tenantBookDigits) instead of a hard-coded *100 check, so a KWD wage payout of 5000.125 is accepted and a 4-decimal amount is rejected. Prospective only: wage payouts already posted through the loan path keep their 1160 journals, their installments, and their unrelieved 2140 accruals — unwinding them is a separate per-tenant remediation decision, not this package. Deliberately unchanged: the pre-transaction withdrawal-balance eligibility gates, the drawer cash movement and treasury record, the manager-approval paths, and the accrual journal's entry date (P1-3, separate).
- INV-PAID-PAYROLL-ITEMS-NEVER-REBUILT (30 October 2026): a paid payroll run item is final and is never recomputed or status-reset by a rebuild. rebuildPayrollRunItems skips an existing item whose status is 'paid' exactly as it already skipped 'approved' items: its stored amounts, its 'paid' status, and its journal link survive any rebuild. The old code recomputed a paid item's money fields and overwrote its status with the rebuild target, which would have resurrected it as payable with a stale journal link — a later payment would then move drawer cash against an already-posted, deduped journal. All rebuild callers were already gated to draft/reviewed runs and no status-back transition exists, so no live path reached a paid item; the guard now lives on the item itself rather than depending on the callers. Deliberately unchanged: the rebuild's release of pending adjustments for the run, the approved-item skip, the excluded-item handling, and every run/item status gate around payment, approval, cancellation, recalculation, review, and editing.
- INV-PAYROLL-ACCRUAL-PERIOD-END-DATE (30 October 2026): the payroll accrual journal is dated at the run's period end (hr_payroll_runs.end_date), never at the approval date — the wage expense belongs to the period the work was performed in. postPayrollAccrual reads the run's end_date and fails closed with HR_PAYROLL_ACCRUAL_PERIOD_END_MISSING (409) when it is missing or unreadable, instead of inventing a date; the period-lock check in insertPostedJournal then rejects approving a run whose period is already closed. The two payment journals (postPayrollPayment, postPayrollItemPayment) deliberately stay dated at the payment date: cash leaves on the day of payment, and backdating them would misstate treasury and bank movements. Deliberately unchanged: every other posting method's entry date, the accrual/payment line algebra, and the recovery worker, which replays the same postPayrollAccrual and inherits the period-end date.
- INV-PAYROLL-EXPORT-REQUIRES-ACCRUAL (31 October 2026): the payroll bank review export (generatePayrollWps) passes only when an hr_payroll_accrual journal exists for the run with status 'posted' and no reversal link — the posting service's own definition of an active journal. A missing accrual fails closed with HR_PAYROLL_EXPORT_ACCRUAL_MISSING (409); a reversed accrual with HR_PAYROLL_EXPORT_ACCRUAL_REVERSED (409). The export must never outrun the ledger: a run whose accrual was never posted, or was reversed after approval, exports no bank file. Deliberately unchanged: the review-only nature of the CSV (certified WPS/SIF generation stays disabled), the run-status gate, the bank-detail and currency checks, repeat exports of a run with an active accrual (no export ledger exists — that is a separate product decision), and the partially-paid run export gate.
- INV-WHT-BASE-EXCLUDES-VAT (31 October 2026): the withholding-tax extraction base is the pre-VAT amount. extractFromPurchases reads the bill's stored tax_amount and prices_include_tax flag and removes the stored VAT for tax-inclusive bills only; tax-exclusive bills already store subtotal pre-VAT, so their base is unchanged. The 300 threshold applies to the extracted base (the SQL pre-filter on gross subtotal can never wrongly exclude a bill, since the base never exceeds the subtotal). Amounts in the extraction round at the tenant's book digits via the canonical engine, never a hard-coded 2. Deliberately unchanged: the single caller-supplied rate/type per extraction run (mixed-type periods need per-bill typing — a separate decision), the service-wide private toMoney helper used by the report and manual entry paths, the created_at period window, and the remittance journal posting.
- INV-VAT-DECLARATION-TENANT-JURISDICTION (31 October 2026): a VAT declaration follows the tenant's own configured jurisdiction, never a caller-supplied country. deriveDeclarationCountry resolves EG/SA from the settings country first and the base currency second (mirroring the HR statutory precedence) and fails closed on an unresolved jurisdiction or a supported-currency tenant outside EG/SA, where no declaration form exists. The request country parameter is ignored. The entity tax number must be the tenant's own registered number from an active tenant_tax_settings row (jurisdiction-matched provider preferred); a missing registration fails closed with VAT_DECLARATION_TAX_ID_MISSING — no fabricated fallback number is ever printed. Deliberately unchanged: document-based aggregation by created_at (a GL-based declaration needs its own account-mapping decision), 2-decimal document rendering (the declaration reports in EGP/SAR), the business-name display fallback, and the frontend country selector (now display-only until its own follow-up removes it).
- INV-ZATCA-NETWORK-FAILURE-NEVER-CLEARS (31 October 2026): a ZATCA network error means ZATCA was never reached, so the submission is 'failed' in every environment, sandbox and simulation included — no simulated clearance or reporting is ever recorded. The sale keeps its built ICV/PIH package (buildZatcaInvoice reuses the stored signed package on retry and does not advance the EGS counter again) and stays in the pending queue for a real retry. Correction to the audit framing: ICV is not burned by a failed submission — the counter advances once at build and the same package is re-sent; only the false clearance on network failure was real. Deliberately unchanged: the live sandbox/production transmission itself, the rejection handling for HTTP error answers, the EGS credential fallbacks, and the stale build-time comment claiming no ZATCA contact exists anywhere (comment-only follow-up).
- INV-MARITIME-SETTLEMENT-RATE-RESOLVED (31 October 2026): createAgentSettlement resolves its exchange rate through resolveFreightExchangeRate, the resolver every other maritime path uses — 1 only for the tenant base currency, a supplied positive rate, or the registered rate for the settlement currency; a missing rate fails closed instead of assuming parity. The settlement currency defaults to USD, so an omitted currency with an omitted rate resolves the registered USD rate. Deliberately unchanged: the rate-history problem (upsertCurrency still overwrites the single live rate row — a history table and as-of conversion are a separate migration package), the FX revaluation policy decision, and the other maritime || fallbacks that are display defaults, not conversion rates.
- INV-ZERO-DIFFERENCE-REVALUATION-NEVER-LOCKS (31 October 2026): only a forex revaluation run that actually posted a journal locks its period/currency (the duplicate guard matches status 'posted'). A zero-difference run posts nothing, so it is recorded with status 'no_difference' for audit, and a later run with a corrected closing rate can still execute. The live-rate update to the closing rate is unchanged. Deliberately unchanged: the sub-threshold aggregation edge (per-line differences below 0.01 summing above 0.01), the rate-history problem, and the FX revaluation policy decision for receivables/payables.
- INV-PDC-RATE-VARIANCE-FLAGGED (31 October 2026): a caller-supplied PDC exchange rate is a contractual term and stands, with two guards in pdcCurrencySnapshot (the single choke point for cheque creation and settlement, foreign currency only — a base-currency supplied rate is ignored by definition). Sanity guard: a supplied rate outside half to double the registered rate fails closed (a USD cheque booked at 1 or 1000 against a registered 48 never posts). Variance flag: a deviation over 5% from the registered rate is accepted but stored flagged_rate_variance=true with registered_exchange_rate alongside (migration 2040000000269, partial index for review listings), so review screens can audit it; a settlement re-rate follows the settlement snapshot and can clear a flag set at creation. With no registered rate there is nothing to compare against, so the supplied rate stands unflagged. Deliberately unchanged: the registered-rate overwrite itself (rate history is a separate migration package); frontend rendering of the flag is a separate follow-up.
- INV-EXCHANGE-RATE-HISTORY (31 October 2026): every registered exchange rate is preserved in the append-only currency_exchange_rate_history table (migration 2040000000270, backfilled from live rows), recorded by a database trigger on genuine rate/base changes — so the upsert, the revaluation closing-rate update, and the currency seed all leave history atomically, and no writer can silently overwrite the past. exchangeRateAsOf reads the rate registered at a moment and fails closed when none was registered yet. Deliberately unchanged: all live readers still use the current rate row; the first as-of consumer is decided by the FX revaluation policy (F4), not by this package.
- INV-PAYMOB-CAPTURE-ONLY (30 October 2026): a Paymob webhook marks an order paid only on a capture transaction, classified by the pure classifyPaymobTransaction engine from Paymob's own flags — never on success/pending alone. Capture means is_capture (capture of a prior authorization) or is_standalone_payment (a one-step sale; this is the proven production shape, with is_capture=false). An authorization-only notification (is_auth, no capture flag) is acknowledged with no state change, so the order stays pending until the capture callback arrives; a void or refund (is_voided/is_refunded, which win over every other flag) on an unpaid order fails the order and releases its reservation through the same compensation as a definitive failure; a successful callback carrying none of the flags is routed to human review as non_capture_transaction, never marked paid outright. The old code accepted every success=true/pending=false callback as a capture, so an authorization hold, a void, or a refund all confirmed the order and its sale. Deliberately unchanged: HMAC verification (still fail-closed first), the already-paid additional-payment review routing (a void/refund arriving after payment still flags review), the definitive-failure branch (success=false), and the amount/currency/session review checks, which still run on capture transactions before payment.
- INV-MARKETPLACE-PREPAID-COLLECTION (30 October 2026): an online order the marketplace already collected converts to a collected invoice, never cash-on-delivery. resolveOnlineOrderCollection treats a paid order as collected when its gateway provider is a supported marketplace (amazon, noon, jumia, trendyol) or its payment method is marketplace_prepaid — the two fields the marketplace sync writes together — on the card channel, with the marketplace named as the provider for the invoice note. A marketplace order that is not yet paid stays COD, exactly like an unpaid gateway order. The card channel is deliberate: it is the only non-cash channel the sale engine understands for a receipt that is not cash in the drawer (postSale books every non-cash payment to the bank side, as for gateway-paid orders); inventing a 'marketplace' channel value would be silently mapped back to 'cash' by normalizeSalePayload, which is the failure this resolver exists to prevent. The old code recognized only gateway/webhook providers, so every synced marketplace order converted as COD and the courier collected the full amount a second time at the door. Deliberately unchanged: the SF-2 sandbox rule (a mock paid order still resolves as COD), the paid-status requirement (SF-3), and the marketplace settlement reconciliation itself — the bulk, sale-unlinked settlement journal (bank net, fees, gross revenue credit) is a separate pre-existing design question, and the sale journal credits revenue under every collection treatment, so this fix changes only the collection side, not the settlement side.
- INV-INVOICE-ALLOCATION-BOOK-PRECISION (30 October 2026): allocateInvoiceDiscount takes the tenant's book precision as a required digits parameter — never a silent 2dp default — and fails closed on any other value. The engine itself was already precision-aware; both sale create/edit paths passed their book digits, but the sales-return legacy-line backfill called it with no digits argument, so stored 3-decimal amounts were forced through a cents conversion and the whole return was blocked with INVALID_INVOICE_ALLOCATION (a stored KWD tax of 10.005 differs from its cents rounding by 0.4999, over the 0.001 tolerance). The returns site now passes the moneyDigits it already derives at the top of createSaleReturn, the two remaining callers pass 2 explicitly, and an omitted or invalid digits value throws INVALID_INVOICE_ALLOCATION instead of rounding silently. Deliberately unchanged: the allocation algebra (proportional shares, last-line absorption, tax-requires-base and discount-exceeds-subtotal guards), the toCents 0.001 float-noise tolerance, and the client-supplied tax rate itself (F1, a separate package: the rate still comes from the payload, only its allocation is now book-precise).
- INV-ZATCA-SIGNED-IMMUTABLE (30 October 2026): a sale carrying a signed ZATCA invoice is immutable in the sales writer — updateSale and cancelSale both reject it with SALE_ZATCA_SIGNED_IMMUTABLE (422) immediately after reading the locked sale row, before any other query or mutation. Signed means zatca_status 'generated' (the signed UBL XML, hash, and QR are stored on the sale and the EGS unit's ICV chain has advanced, with no reset path) or 'cleared'/'reported'/'warning' (ZATCA accepted the invoice); editing or cancelling after any of these would leave a signed attestation describing superseded amounts while the replacement consumed a second ICV for the same sale. 'not_submitted', 'failed', and 'rejected' stay editable, so a rejected invoice can still be corrected and re-signed. The guard is the shared pure assertSaleNotZatcaSigned helper (the status vocabulary lives with the ZATCA module, not duplicated in the writer), and it sits beside the existing exchange-immutability and status guards in both methods. Deliberately unchanged: the ZATCA builder and submission flows (the hardcoded 15% recomputation, the sandbox cleared-on-error marking, and the ICV-consumed-at-build behaviour are separate packages F4/F5), the fiscal-period locks (which already block edits in closed periods), and the missing correction flows — there is still no credit-note flow and no void/regenerate path for a generated-but-unsubmitted invoice, so a merchant who spots a typo after signing currently has no compliant correction route; both need product decisions and are separate items, not reasons to leave signed invoices editable.
- INV-IMPORT-PROFORMA-EXCHANGE-RATE (30 October 2026): converting an approved proforma invoice into a purchase order costs its lines at the proforma's own contract exchange rate — never an invented one. generatePoFromProforma uses rate 1 for EGP proformas and the stored exchange_rate_contract otherwise, and throws IMPORT_EXCHANGE_RATE_REQUIRED when the contract rate is missing, zero, or negative, before any supplier lookup, purchase-order insert, or proforma update. The old code fell back to a hardcoded 50 in exactly that case and recorded the invented rate only in the draft PO's free-text notes, so approving the draft posted purchase costs, stock values, and COGS at a rate nobody chose. Deliberately unchanged: the feasibility and quotation estimate defaults (exchangeRateCommercial || 50 in planning estimates — a merchant-visible estimate reviewed at feasibility stage, whose default is a separate product decision; the carry-over of that estimate into a stored contract rate is now closed by INV-FEASIBILITY-CONTRACT-RATE-REQUIRED below); the shipment landed-cost summary's own || 50 (a read path whose missing-rate treatment needs its own decision — flagging vs failing — not a cost-creation gate); the EGP-as-1 shortcut (kept as-is; a non-EGP base-currency tenant's same-currency proforma without a rate now fails closed and the merchant sets rate 1 explicitly, which is the safe direction); and the live-rate table (no fallback to a stored live rate was added — which rate source is authoritative for proforma conversion is a separate decision).
- INV-FEASIBILITY-CONTRACT-RATE-REQUIRED (31 October 2026): convertFeasibilityToProject uses rate 1 for EGP-budget projects and the feasibility study's own exchangeRateCommercial otherwise, and throws IMPORT_EXCHANGE_RATE_REQUIRED when the commercial rate is missing, zero, negative, or non-finite, before any proforma insert or project update. The old code fell back to a hardcoded 50 in exactly that case and stored it as the proforma's exchange_rate_contract while pricing the whole cash-flow schedule at it, so the invented rate became the authoritative contract rate that the proforma-to-PO gate then treated as chosen. Deliberately unchanged: the quotation estimate default (dto.exchangeRate || 50 in createQuotation, a merchant-reviewed planning document); the shipment-item estimate seed and the container-autopsy read path (both display/estimate, recomputed or read-only, not contract rates); and no fallback to the stored live rate (which rate source is authoritative remains a separate decision). Prospective only: proformas already created carrying 50 as their contract rate are not rewritten — they are caught by the proforma gate if their rate is ever cleared, and otherwise stand as merchant-approved records.
- INV-VAN-SETTLE-LOCKED-RECHECK (30 October 2026): settleTrip re-reads the trip row with FOR UPDATE and re-checks its status as the first statement inside the settlement transaction, before any stock read, deduction, unload transfer, or journal. A trip already 'settled' there is rejected with TRIP_ALREADY_SETTLED (409); a missing row fails closed with the existing TRIP_NOT_FOUND. The pre-transaction status check on the unlocked read is kept as the fast path for the common case (a second, sequential settle still gets TRIP_NOT_FOUND there), but it is not the concurrency guard: without the locked re-check, two concurrent settles both passed the pre-check and both ran the stock deductions and unload transfers, while only the journal was saved — postVanTripSettlement skips a duplicate by its SELECT guard on the existing journal, so the second settle committed duplicated stock effects with no journal at all. The row lock serializes concurrent settlers, so the second waits for the first transaction and then sees 'settled'. Deliberately unchanged: the settlement algebra (cash variance, stock audit reconciliation, unload transfers, journal contents), the maker-checker and odometer validations (which still run before the transaction), the 7b race-window unique index (kept as the backstop for the journal itself), and the sequential double-settle response (TRIP_NOT_FOUND from the pre-check, unchanged). Not covered here: the remaining van F2/F3/F5 findings (stale trip counters after sale edit/cancel, unlinked field returns, concurrent refund approvals) — separate packages.
- INV-VAN-RAPID-FIRE-SIGNATURE (30 October 2026): the database rapid-fire backstop in executeFieldSale deduplicates only a recent sale whose product/quantity multiset is identical to the new payload's (sorted productId:qty signatures compared), never a sale that merely shares the line count. The old check compared only the number of sale_items rows, so a genuinely different sale to the same customer within 4 seconds — different products, same line count — was silently swallowed and reported as created with the first sale's id and cashPaid 0, with no second sale, stock movement, or journal. Prices are deliberately excluded from the signature: the server may adjust stored unit prices (tiers/offers), so comparing stored prices against raw payload prices would miss true double-taps whose prices were adjusted on the first write; the in-process fingerprint layers (in-flight map + 5s cache, keyed on the full productId:qty:unitPrice signature) already cover the price dimension, and the clientTxId check still takes precedence over this backstop. Deliberately unchanged: the 4-second window, the trip/rep/customer scoping, the clientTxId and in-process layers, and the twin rapid-fire check in the field-collection path (customer + amount + 4s, with no trip/rep/method column on customer_payments to scope it by — narrowing it needs a schema or idempotency-key decision and is a separate package, recorded as the immediate follow-up to this one).
- INV-VAN-RETURN-APPROVAL-PROVENANCE (30 October 2026): approving a field return re-validates its provenance against current data inside the approval transaction — it never trusts the submit-time checks. approveFieldReturn locks the trip row (FOR UPDATE, before any provenance read, serializing concurrent approvals for the trip and against settleTrip) and requires the trip still 'open'; then it re-runs the submit-time provenance rules on the transaction: a linked return requires its sale to exist, still be 'posted', belong to the same customer and rep, and leave enough un-returned quantity per line at or below the line's net unit price (+0.05); an unlinked return requires the rep's posted sales to the customer to still cover the quantity per product at or below the highest net unit charged (+0.05). Already-returned quantities count approved and pending_approval returns excluding the return being approved (it would otherwise count itself). A violation fails closed with the submit-time error code (RETURN_QTY_EXCEEDS_SOLD, RETURN_PRICE_EXCEEDS_NET, RETURN_SALE_NOT_POSTED, SALE_CUSTOMER_MISMATCH, SALE_REP_MISMATCH, NO_REP_SALES_HISTORY_FOR_RETURN) before any stock movement, customer credit, counter update, or journal; the request stays pending_approval for a human to reject or re-examine, and no fraud-radar event is recorded at approval time (a changed basis is often a legitimate sale edit, not attempted fraud). Deliberately unchanged: the submit-time validation and its fraud-radar events (kept exactly as the first gate); the approval's stock, customer-credit, counter, and journal effects once provenance passes; and the linked-path already-returned counting, which still counts only items carrying a saleItemId (prior returns submitted without one remain invisible to the per-line map — the same hole submit-time had — and are a separate follow-up, as is the unlinked aggregate's tolerance for returns against sales on other trips, which the aggregate check deliberately covers).
- INV-SALE-TAX-RATE-CONFIGURED-OR-ZERO (30 October 2026): a sale's tax rate is the tenant's configured rate or 0 (exempt) — never whatever the client sent. createSale and updateSale resolve the rate through the pure resolveSaleTaxRate engine against the settings-map `taxRate` (legacy `tax_rate` spelling as fallback, mirroring the held-draft bill formatter's defensive read), loaded through the existing cached getTenantSettingsMap with both spellings added to SALE_SETTING_KEYS; a supplied rate that matches neither the configured rate (within 0.001) nor 0 is rejected with SALE_TAX_RATE_MISMATCH (400) before any totals, stock, or journal work, and the resolved rate replaces the payload rate so the stored tax_rate and the computed totals agree exactly. An absent or unparseable configured rate behaves as 0 (the activation default and the frontend's `|| 0` fallback), so only exempt sales pass until the tenant configures a rate; an omitted payload rate normalizes to 0 upstream and is therefore exempt.
