Deliberately unchanged: purchases (a purchase tax rate is the supplier invoice's own rate, never the tenant's — purchases-write untouched); van field sales (which carry no tax rate); storefront order conversion (which sends no rate and therefore converts at 0, exactly as before); pricesIncludeTax handling; and the ZATCA builder's hardcoded 15% and the ETA serializer's 14% (separate F4/F8 findings). No per-line exemption concept exists in the codebase, so 0 is the only permitted deviation from the configured rate — requiring an explicit rate, or per-line exemption flags, is a separate product decision.
- INV-VAN-TRIP-COUNTER-ADJUSTMENT (30 October 2026): editing or cancelling a field sale moves its trip's counters with it. updateSale and cancelSale lock the sale's trip row (FOR UPDATE, right after the locked sale row is read and before any stock work — the order settleTrip uses) and adjust the four creation-time counters (sales_amount, cash_collected, credit_sales, total_instapay_sales) by the contribution delta: cancelSale reverses the old sale's contribution in full; updateSale applies new-minus-old once the replacement rows exist. The contribution is computed by the shared pure tripSaleContribution helper, which mirrors the five creation branches in executeFieldSaleInternal (cash / card / instapay incl. vodafone_cash and bank_transfer channels / credit / split), reading the split's cash/credit division from the tender rows when the generic writer wrote them, from the sale_credit ledger row when the field flow wrote it, and from paid_amount only as a last resort. A settled trip's counters are history and are never moved; a sale with no trip touches no trip row. Deliberately unchanged: the creation-time increment branches themselves, the collection and return counter paths (which already maintained the counters), the settleTrip expected-cash derivation (still the counter, now kept current), and the customer-ledger sale_credit row (read, never rewritten, by this package). Honest limits: the delta approach is exact from this package forward — sales edited or cancelled BEFORE it keep whatever staleness they already caused, which needs a separate one-time reconciliation decision (recompute-from-ledger), not an automatic repair; and updateSale's late delta application is verified by the shared helper's table plus the early-lock wiring test, not by an end-to-end drive of the full edit flow in the clone (the same class of limit as the frozen-cost package — the live-Postgres acceptance run must cover an edited field sale).
- INV-DOCUMENT-TAX-ATTESTS-POSTED-INVOICE (30 October 2026): a signed tax document attests the posted invoice — it never recomputes tax. buildZatcaInvoice derives every line through the pure buildDocumentTax engine (tax-integration/engines/document-tax.engine.ts): a line with stored allocations uses net_line_total as its ex-tax base and allocated_tax as its tax, rounded to document precision (UBL: 2) with the canonical rounder, so the document's subtotal, tax total, and payable equal the sum of its lines exactly; a legacy line without allocations falls back to the STORED sale.tax_rate only (extracting tax when prices_include_tax, adding it otherwise), never a hardcoded rate; and the document-level <cbc:Percent> comes from the lines' own rate — the old builder hardcoded 15% for the amounts and the percent, so an exempt sale signed as 15%, a 14% sale signed as 15%, and a tax-inclusive sale signed at 15% of its tax-inclusive total. The engine fails closed on a non-finite or negative stored rate, an empty line list, or corrupt stored allocations. Deliberately unchanged: the posting gate's ownership of rate lawfulness (Tax F1) — the signer attests what was posted, including a legacy wrong-but-posted rate, and does not re-validate it against settings; the UBL 2-decimal rendering itself (the GL keeps book precision as the authority, so a 3-decimal posted tax may differ from its document rendering by a half minor unit); the line PriceAmount (still the gross unit price — the pre-existing PriceAmount/LineExtensionAmount discount seam is untouched); the EGS/ICV/PIH chain, QR, and submission flows (F5 sandbox marking and ICV-consumed-at-build are separate items); and the ETA serializer's hardcoded 14% (the next consumer of this engine, a separate package — the engine is shared, the migration is per consumer).
- INV-ETA-DOCUMENT-TAX-ATTESTS-POSTED-INVOICE (30 October 2026): the ETA document attests the posted invoice through the same shared buildDocumentTax engine as the ZATCA builder — it never recomputes tax. serializeSaleToEtaFormat derives every line from the posted allocations (net_line_total as the taxable base, allocated_tax as the T1 amount, the stored sale.tax_rate as the T1 rate) at document precision (2), with the legacy fallback to the stored rate only (extracting tax when prices_include_tax); document netAmount, totalTax, and totalAmount are the engine's subtotal, vatTotal, and totalWithVat, so the document totals equal the sum of its lines exactly, and the line itemsDiscount now reports the stored allocated discount instead of a hardcoded 0 (so salesTotal minus itemsDiscount equals the reported netTotal on discounted lines). The old serializer hardcoded 14% of the gross line total, so an exempt sale was reported at 14%, a 5% sale at 14%, and a tax-inclusive sale at 14% of its tax-inclusive total. Deliberately unchanged: the posting gate's ownership of rate lawfulness (the serializer attests what was posted, as the ZATCA builder does); the EGP currencySold / exchangeRate 1 hardcodes (ETA is an EGP-denominated regime and the serializer is Egypt-specific — a non-EGP tenant submitting to ETA is a misconfiguration, not a serializer decision); the unitValue amountEGP (still the gross unit price, as in the ZATCA line PriceAmount seam); the V009 tax subtype on every line (the subtype taxonomy for 0% lines is a regulatory mapping decision, not a rate decision — a 0% line currently keeps the standard subtype with rate 0); the document-level totalDiscountAmount (still the sale discount), the issuer/receiver blocks, and the submission flow itself (the empty-arrays fallback that marks invoices submitted is a separate F8 item, untouched here — this package migrates the serializer only).
- INV-VAN-REFUND-CUSTODY-GUARD (30 October 2026): a cash refund is paid out of a trip's collected cash only if the balance, freshly re-read inside the approval transaction under the trip FOR UPDATE lock, covers it — so two concurrent cash-refund approvals serialize: the second waits for the lock and then sees the first approval's committed deduction, and the deduction itself is a relative SQL update (cash_collected - totalAmount), exact under the lock. The serialization was already in place from the F3 trip lock; what this package closes is the tolerance the guard allowed: the sufficiency tolerance is now one minor unit of the tenant's book currency (derived through the canonical tenantBaseCurrency helper on the cash branch only), never a hardcoded 0.01 — 0.01 is ten minor units on a 3-decimal tenant and let a refund overdraw the rep's custody by up to that much even sequentially, with no concurrency at all. Custody is per trip (no rep-level cash balance exists); credit refunds never touch cash_collected; sale edits and cancels take the same trip lock (F2), and settleTrip takes it too (F1), so no other writer can move the balance between the check and the deduction except through relative updates. Deliberately unchanged: the submit-time advisory check in submitFieldReturn and the pure evaluateFieldReturnGovernance evaluator, which keep their own hardcoded 0.01 — the approval-time guard re-checks strictly, so the submit check can only admit a request the guard then rejects, never the reverse; the provenance re-validation, stock, credit, and journal effects of approval; and the sequential double-approval response (a second approval of the SAME return still fails RETURN_ALREADY_PROCESSED on its locked row). The live-Postgres acceptance run must cover two genuinely concurrent cash-refund approvals against one trip balance — the mock-DB spec pins the lock ordering and the per-approval fresh read, not real lock contention.
- INV-THREE-WAY-MATCH-BILL-VAT-RATE (30 October 2026): the three-way-match gate verifies a bill at the bill's own stored VAT rate — never a default. verifyPurchaseThreeWayMatch passes the purchase row's tax_rate (percent, converted to the engine's fraction) and its prices_include_tax flag into computeThreeWayMatch; the engine's vatRate is now a required input and fails closed on a missing, non-finite, or negative rate instead of silently defaulting to 0.14, so an exempt bill no longer reports 14% VAT and an inflated payable, and a 15% bill is no longer understated. For tax-inclusive bills the VAT leg extracts the tax (lineTotal - lineTotal / (1 + rate)) and the payable is the invoiced total itself — adding the extracted VAT again would double-count it — while the GRNI/PPV legs stay on the stored gross basis (bills store effectiveUnitCost as sent, and POs carry the same gross basis, so price variance and reconciliation are basis-consistent either way). Deliberately unchanged: the matching, quantity, price-tolerance, service-certificate, and override rules; the per-product vatRateByProduct fallback (still available for mixed-rate invoices, though no caller passes it today); the purchase rate itself (a purchase rate is the supplier invoice's own rate — Tax F1 deliberately left purchases untouched, and this gate attests the bill as received rather than re-validating its rate against settings); and the reconciliation discrepancy rule (VAT never participated in it). The two pre-existing engine spec files gained explicit vatRate: 0.14 arguments (17 insertions, no assertion changed) because they relied on the removed default.
- INV-WAC-UNIFIED-BLEND (30 October 2026): one weighted-average cost policy for every receipt path. importWeightedAverageCost now delegates the blend to the canonical weightedReceiptCost (INV-NEGATIVE-STOCK-WAC) instead of its own formula that took the incoming cost whenever the prior stock was zero or negative — a receipt following overselling (-10 @ 5, then 20 @ 6) now blends to 7 instead of erasing the booked shortfall value at 6, and the same receipt is valued identically whether it arrives as a purchase or as an import. The import-side validation and BadRequestException contract are preserved (corrupt stored cost or value still fail closed with the same Arabic 400; a zero-quantity group keeps the old cost, which the canonical engine would reject), and the import receipt caller now recovers its before-quantity through the canonical recoverWacBeforeQty helper (identical math to the inline expression it replaced, so the quantity source is one everywhere). Deliberately unchanged: the late-expense additive bump in the import flow (a revaluation of stock already on hand by an additional value, not a receipt — it needs its own revaluation primitive in the landed-cost unification, it cannot be expressed as a receipt blend); the direct cost_price writes of the purchase, goods-receipt, and import paths (converging receipt writes onto valueStockReceipt is a separate package); and already-posted import receipts (prospective only — a receipt that already stored 6 on negative stock keeps 6 until the next receipt re-blends it).
- INV-FIELD-COLLECTION-TWIN-SCOPE (30 October 2026): a field collection is identified on customer_payments by trip + rep + channel + client transaction id, not by customer + amount alone. Migration 2040000000268 adds nullable van_trip_id, rep_id, and client_tx_id columns (no foreign keys, matching customer_ledger.van_trip_id), backfills client_tx_id from the legacy [tx:...] note tag, and indexes the twin-check predicate; the writer stores all three plus the mapped payment_channel (cash/instapay/vodafone_cash/bank_transfer onto the 0251 check-constraint vocabulary cash/instapay/wallet/bank, omitted method meaning cash as the posting already assumed). The database rapid-fire backstop now matches the same trip, rep, channel, customer, and amount within 4 seconds — a second legitimate collection on another trip, by another rep, or on another channel inside the window is no longer swallowed and reported as the first — and the clientTxId lookup checks the client_tx_id column first, falling back to the note tag only for rows the backfill could not parse. The in-process fingerprint and 30s/5s caches already keyed on trip + rep and are unchanged; the note tag keeps being written. Deliberately unchanged: non-van writers of customer_payments (installments, purchase-side payments) leave the new columns NULL and are untouched by the scoped check; legacy rows with NULL scope columns can never false-positive the scoped predicates; no unique constraint on client_tx_id (a retried client transaction id across tenants or customers is not a database error — the application lookup is tenant- and customer-scoped, and the tag fallback must keep working for unparsed history).
- INV-ROUND-MONEY-COLLISION-RESOLVED (30 October 2026): the name `roundMoney` belongs to exactly one function — the canonical money-minor-units engine rounder, which takes the tenant's book digits (2 or 3) and throws on non-finite input. The legacy one-argument, hard-coded-2-decimal, lenient rounder in common/utils/financial-integrity.ts is renamed roundMoneyToTwoDecimals (definition, its three uses inside validateSalePayments, the financial-integrity spec, and the two e2e simulations that imported it), so no future importer or auto-import can silently grab the 2-decimal rounder for a posting or a stored amount. Zero behavior change: every renamed call site computes exactly what it computed before, and validateSalePayments still answers in 2 decimals (its KWD 10.125 case still returns paidAmount 10.13) — giving it tenant digits is the money program's next package, not this rename. Deliberately unchanged: the four module-private roundMoney helpers (rep-target commissions, returns-write helper with its digits = 2 default, storefront pricing and offers engines) — they share no export scope with the canonical engine, so they cannot collide on import; each needs its own triage in the money program before any rename.
- INV-RETURN-HELPER-DIGITS-REQUIRED (30 October 2026): the returns-write helper never rounds at an assumed precision. `digits` is REQUIRED on buildSaleReturnLine, buildPurchaseReturnLine, calculateReturnDocumentTotal, and calculateNextLedgerBalance (and on the file-private rounder) — the silent `= 2` default is gone, so a caller that forgets the tenant's book digits fails closed (the canonical engine throws on an undefined digits) instead of silently cutting a 3-decimal tenant's return lines, totals, and ledger balances to cents. Zero behavior change: every live caller in returns.service.ts already passes book digits, and the existing infra spec now passes explicit 2s with every assertion unchanged, including the KWD 0.001 cases at digits 3. Deliberately unchanged: originalPurchaseReturnUnitCost (fixed 6-decimal WAC precision, no digits concept) and the three remaining module-private roundMoney helpers (rep-target, storefront pricing/offers) — the storefront pair is now closed by INV-STOREFRONT-PRICING-DIGITS below; only the rep-target helper still needs its own triage.
- INV-STOREFRONT-PRICING-DIGITS (30 October 2026): storefront prices are never rounded at an assumed precision. `digits` is REQUIRED on resolveOrderLinePrice (requestedVariant is now a defaulted `= null` parameter, because a required parameter cannot follow an optional one), calculateOfferAdjustedPrice, and resolveBestStorefrontOffer (todayIso is now a defaulted parameter for the same reason) — the two file-private 2-decimal rounders are deleted and every price rounds through the canonical engine at the caller's digits, so a KWD variant at 10.125 and a 10% offer at 9.113 stay exact instead of being cut to cents before the shopper sees a total. The storefront service derives the digits from the same currency source the order functions already read (settings currency, then tenant currency, then EGP) via storefrontMoneyDigits, passed at all eight call sites (catalog projection, catalog page, quote, create, update); an unreadable currency string keeps the historical 2-decimal display for public reads while the posting path still throws on it. Behavior change ONLY on 3-decimal tenants, and only toward book precision: 2-decimal tenants compute exactly what they computed before (the three existing specs pass explicit 2s with every assertion unchanged). Deliberately unchanged: the frontend variant-pricing mirror (features/storefront/lib/storefront-variant-pricing.ts), which still rounds at 2 decimals, so the displayed price can differ from the charged price on a 3-decimal tenant until the frontend follows the backend; the line-total roundFactor math — now closed by INV-STOREFRONT-LINE-TOTAL-DIGITS below (the update path had its own hard-coded /100 line total, also closed there); the POS offer engine in sales-write.helper.ts — now closed by INV-POS-OFFER-DIGITS below; the rep-target commissions rounder — now closed by INV-REP-COMMISSION-DIGITS below, the last module-private roundMoney helper; discount percents, which stay plain integers.
- INV-POS-OFFER-DIGITS (30 October 2026): POS offer prices are never rounded at an assumed precision. `digits` is REQUIRED through the whole sales-write offer chain — calculateOfferAdjustedPrice, pickBestApplicableOffer, and calculateAllowedSaleUnitPrice (new required moneyDigits params field) — and on roundCurrency itself, calculateCollectibleTotal, calculatePaidAmount, and buildPreparedSaleItem's options.moneyDigits (now a required options field, no default object), so a KWD offer at 9.113 stays exact and the best-offer CHOICE is made on book-precision prices: a fixed 1.02 offer and a 10% offer tie at 9.11 in cents (tie-break picks the percent offer) but at 3 decimals the fixed offer wins outright at 9.105 vs 9.113. The service passes its moneyDigits at all three calculateAllowedSaleUnitPrice sites (create, update, saveHeldSale — the last gained its own tenantBaseCurrency derivation, mirroring the other two); buildPreparedSaleItem's two service sites already passed moneyDigits. Behavior change ONLY on 3-decimal tenants, and only toward book precision: 2-decimal tenants compute exactly what they computed before (the 2-decimal branch then kept its exact historical EPSILON formula; it has since been converged onto the canonical rounder by INV-CANONICAL-ROUNDING-EVERYWHERE below, and the two existing specs pass explicit 2s with every assertion unchanged). Deliberately unchanged: resolveSalePayments' cents splitting (Math.round(*100), a separate payment-splitting package) and calculatePaidAmount/resolveSalePayments having no live callers (dead helpers; calculatePaidAmount's digits were made required for file consistency only); quantity precision (toFixed(3) on requiredQty/afterQty is quantities, not money); the frontend POS offer mirror, which still rounds at 2 decimals until the frontend follows the backend; the rep-target commissions rounder — now closed by INV-REP-COMMISSION-DIGITS below, the last module-private roundMoney helper.
- INV-REP-COMMISSION-DIGITS (30 October 2026): rep commissions and rep-isolated customer balances are never rounded at an assumed precision. `digits` is REQUIRED on all four money functions in rep-target.engine.ts — calculateRepCommissionMetrics (new required moneyDigits input field), aggregateCustomerBalancesByRep (defaulted second parameter, so the required digits may follow it), resolveIsolatedRepCustomerBalance and validateIsolatedRepCollection (new required moneyDigits params fields) — and the file-private 2-decimal rounder is DELETED, the last module-private money rounder in the money program: every one of its 27 uses now rounds through the canonical engine at the caller's digits, so a KWD commission of 10.125, a remaining payable of 15.188, and a rep balance of 10.124 stay exact instead of being cut to cents, and a sub-cent credit leg is no longer dropped from the aggregation. The old rounder also swallowed non-finite values to 0 commission; the canonical engine fails closed on them. The services derive the digits from the tenant's base currency and pass the same value down the whole chain: van-targets (both commission sites; the list site hoists the derivation above its sync map callback, since await is illegal there), van-routes and van-sales (each getCustomerRepBalancesMap site derives once and reuses the value for the isolated/validation calls that read the map), partners (both customer-list sites), and getCustomerRepBalancesMap itself takes a required moneyDigits option and forwards it. Behavior change ONLY on 3-decimal tenants, and only toward book precision, except for floating-point half-cent edge cases at 2 decimals, where the canonical rounder (plain Math.round) can differ from the old EPSILON formula by one minor unit — no pinned 2-decimal value in the existing specs sits on such an edge (all seven affected specs pass with every assertion unchanged). Deliberately unchanged: calculateRepTargetMetrics' whole-unit remaining/required-daily outputs and its one-decimal rates — now closed as INV-REP-TARGET-METRICS-DIGITS below for the inputs only, with the whole-unit outputs deliberately kept; the frontend rep-target displays, which still show 2 decimals until the frontend follows the backend.
- INV-STOREFRONT-LINE-TOTAL-DIGITS (30 October 2026): storefront order totals are never rounded at an assumed precision or an incomplete currency list. The hand-rolled `roundFactor` math in quoteOnlineOrder and createOnlineOrder (a 3-currency list — KWD/BHD/OMR only) and the hard-coded `/100` line total in updateCustomerOrder are all gone: each function derives one `moneyDigits` from storefrontMoneyDigits (settings currency, then tenant currency, then EGP; the same helper the pricing engines already use) and rounds every total through the canonical engine, so a JOD/TND/IQD/LYD quote of 10.125 stays 10.125 instead of being cut to 10.13 while the priced line already showed 10.125. The shared resolveOrderCharges coupon discount — which feeds all three totals — rounds at the same digits from the settings currency (the only currency source available inside that helper), instead of its hard-coded /100. The WhatsApp display formatter in createOnlineOrder keeps its EGP whole-number special case and takes its 3-decimal branch from the same digits value (display only). The rounding formula itself is unchanged (Math.round at the factor), so KWD/BHD/OMR and 2-decimal tenants compute exactly what they computed before; the only change is the four missing 3-decimal currencies. The stored/response total_amount in create/update stays raw arithmetic over rounded lines plus charges, exactly as before — only the quote response rounds its totals, as it always did. Deliberately unchanged: the frontend variant-pricing mirror; the delivery fee, which is a configured value read raw from settings/zone, not computed. The getActiveTableBill service-charge/tax/tip math is now closed by INV-TABLE-BILL-DIGITS-AND-RATE-VALIDATION below.
- INV-REP-TARGET-METRICS-DIGITS (30 October 2026): rep target tracking inputs are never normalized at an assumed precision. `moneyDigits` is REQUIRED on calculateRepTargetMetrics, and its three money input normalizations (sales target, sales actual, collections actual) round through the canonical engine at the caller's digits, so a 3-decimal tenant's target of 100000.125 is reported as 100000.125 instead of 100000.13, and — the sharper edge — a rep 0.001 short of target (99999.999 against 100000) is no longer reported as having achieved it, because the achieved comparison now runs on book-precision inputs. The services derive the digits from the tenant's base currency (van-targets derives once per method and reuses it; the list site reuses the commission derivation hoisted above its sync map callback; van-sales derives in the trip view). Deliberately kept, by an explicit user decision after investigation (2026-10-30): the remaining/required-daily outputs stay whole currency units (Math.round / Math.ceil) and the rates stay one-decimal percents — a display convention the frontend duplicates when it renders them (the admin tab re-rounds with Math.round/Math.ceil itself), and no consumer persists these outputs or feeds them into journals or commissions. The collection target stays whole-rounded as before: targets are set in whole units. Behavior change ONLY on 3-decimal tenants, and only in the input echoes and the achieved comparisons; 2-decimal tenants compute exactly what they computed before.
- INV-TRIP-RECONCILIATION-DIGITS (30 October 2026): van trip reconciliation amounts are never rounded at an assumed precision. `moneyDigits` is REQUIRED on reconcileTripFinancials (new required input field; all 20 amounts — loaded, sales, cash/credit, collections, returns, refunds, expenses, counted cash, remaining stock value, goods-equation and cash-variance intermediates, instapay legs — round through the canonical engine) and `digits` is REQUIRED on reconcileVanStockAudit (all 5 shortage/overage cost amounts round through the canonical engine), and both file-private 2-decimal rounders are DELETED, so a 3-decimal tenant's shortage cost of 10.125 stays 10.125 instead of being cut to 10.13. The stock-audit quantities stay at 4 decimals (they are quantities, not money). The 0.05 goods-equation tolerance and the 0.01 cash-variance status band in reconcileTripFinancials are policy bands, deliberately UNCHANGED: a 0.005 cash variance still reads 'balanced' at 3 decimals; only the amounts changed, and the bands are an open policy decision for when the engine is wired live. The single permitted import in the engine file is the canonical money engine (pure computation, no I/O); the file's purity invariant otherwise stands. The live settleTrip caller derives the digits from the tenant's base currency inside its settlement transaction and passes them to reconcileVanStockAudit, so the rep shortage charge reads book-precision costs from this package forward. HONEST LIMIT: reconcileTripFinancials has no production caller today — it is imported by van-sales.service.ts but never invoked, and the live settleTrip path computes its own cash variance inline with its own toFixed(2) math (a separate package); correcting the engine changes no live settlement behavior until it is wired. The credit-limit, trip-expense, field-return, and odometer evaluators in the same file keep their own toFixed(2) math and are separate packages, not this one. Behavior change ONLY on 3-decimal tenants, and only toward book precision.
- INV-PAYROLL-PAYMENT-CHANNEL-ACCOUNTS (31 October 2026): whole-run and per-item payroll payment postings map the payment channel to the same account — 'bank' pays from bank 1120, every other channel ('cash', 'drawer') from cash 1110, in both postPayrollPayment and postPayrollItemPayment. The whole-run posting previously sent 'drawer' to 1120 although payPayrollRun takes the money out of the cashier drawer. Prospective only: runs already paid from the drawer carry a 1120 credit that must be reclassified per tenant, not rewritten.
- INV-ZATCA-SELLER-IDENTITY-AND-PROFILE (31 October 2026): a signed ZATCA document attests the tenant's own configured identity and the correct processing profile, never hardcoded demo data. The seller VAT number comes from the active ZATCA_SAUDI tax-settings row and must be 15 digits; the fabricated '300000000000003' fallback is deleted, and a missing, inactive, or malformed tax ID fails closed before the ICV chain advances. The seller name follows companyName, then storeName, then the tenant business name. ProfileID follows the invoice type: clearance:1.0 for standard (B2B) invoices, reporting:1.0 for simplified (B2C) — matching the submission endpoints. Deliberately unchanged: the structured seller address has no configured source (settings hold one free-text address only), so the address block stays hardcoded until a structured address settings surface exists; the customer VAT fallback and the client-side-only validateCompliance check are separate follow-ups.
- INV-ETA-EMPTY-RESPONSE-NEVER-SUBMITS (31 October 2026): only documents ETA names as accepted become 'submitted' and only named rejections become 'failed'. A submission answer with empty accepted and rejected arrays changes no invoice status — every invoice stays pending/failed for retry or verification via checkSubmissionStatus — and the submission id comes from ETA or is null, never invented locally. Deliberately unchanged: the status-check and document-detail endpoints, which already fail closed on non-OK answers.
- INV-TAX-DOCUMENT-RATE-SINGLE-SOURCE (31 October 2026): the document-level tax rate has exactly one source. buildDocumentTax attests the stored invoice rate once; buildZatcaInvoice states it as documentVatRate and the UBL renderer prints it verbatim, never re-deriving it from the first line; the ETA serializer consumes the same engine totals. A document whose stated rate disagrees with a line rate signs the stated rate at document level. Deliberately unchanged: per-line rates still ride on each line, and the posting gate (configured rate or zero) still owns lawfulness, never the signer.
- INV-CONFIGURED-TAX-ID-SINGLE-SOURCE (31 October 2026): one pure rule selects the tenant's configured tax row for every tax consumer — the VAT declaration, the ZATCA signer, and the ETA serializer. Exact provider match wins; the declaration alone falls back to the first non-blank row, while ZATCA and ETA never borrow another jurisdiction's registration, and a blank or inactive matched row is returned as-is so each consumer's own fail-closed message survives. Selection never validates: the ZATCA identity engine, the declaration's 409, and the ETA settings error still own their failures.
- INV-WAC-SINGLE-WRITER (31 October 2026): every stock receipt blends through the one writer, valueStockReceipt — quantity recovery plus the algebraic blend plus the write. The purchase, goods-receipt, and import receipt paths call it instead of re-implementing read-blend-write; a zero-quantity line writes nothing. The writer never touches updated_at. Deliberately unchanged: the landed-cost and import revaluation bumps (additive revaluation of an existing pool, not a receipt blend), catalog pricing assignment (applySuggestedPrices), per-serial and per-line cost snapshots, and product creation.
- INV-STOCK-POOL-REVALUATION (31 October 2026): a late cost landing on stock already on hand is a pool revaluation, not a receipt blend — the pure revalueStockPool primitive spreads the additional value over the existing pool as (qty * cost + additional) / qty at 6dp, failing closed on a missing or non-positive pool, a negative cost, or a negative addition; the revalueStockCost writer applies it and stamps updated_at. The landed-cost bump and the late shipment expense bump both use it; each caller keeps its own quantity policy and its own guard. Deliberately unchanged: the lot and shipment-item SQL increments (no lot lock is held in that flow), catalog pricing assignment, and the receipt blend itself.
- INV-POSTING-DIGITS-REQUIRED (31 October 2026): no silent 2dp default anywhere on the posting boundary — the posting service's private toMoney, maxAllowedDifference, and addLine take the tenant's book digits as a required argument, and tsc rejects any call that omits it. The removal surfaced 12 call sites in postService, postManufacturingWorkOrder, and postWithholdingTaxRemittance that had relied on the default while each method already derived bookDigits; all 12 now pass it, so their journal lines round at book digits (a 3dp service amount, finished-goods cost, or withheld tax keeps its third decimal instead of being re-rounded to 2dp). The commercial-posting spec's direct addLine calls were updated the same way. Prospective only: journals already posted at 2dp in those three methods are not rewritten.
- INV-OPENING-BALANCE-BOOK-PRECISION (31 October 2026): a partner opening balance is recorded at the tenant's book digits — addCustomerOpeningBalance and addSupplierOpeningBalance round the amount with the canonical roundMoney at the digits of the tenant's base currency, and the same rounded amount is written to the ledger row, the partner balance, and the amount handed to the posting service, which already re-derived book digits. The old hardcoded toFixed(2) wrote 100.13 everywhere for a 3dp tenant opening at 100.125, including the posted journal. Prospective only: opening balances already recorded at 2dp are not rewritten.
- INV-CANONICAL-ROUNDING-EVERYWHERE (31 October 2026): money rounding has one formula — the canonical engine's roundMoney at the tenant's book digits. The EPSILON half-up branches are gone from the purchases-write, sales-write, and returns-write helpers and from the GRNI settlement minor-unit conversion: each now delegates to (or, for GRNI minor units, uses the identical rounding step of) the canonical engine, so a binary half-cent just below the half (1.005) rounds down to 1.00 at 2 digits instead of up to 1.01. Behavior change is exactly that one minor unit on such edges, at 2 digits only; 3-digit paths already used the canonical engine and are unchanged, and previously pinned non-edge answers (4.255 to 4.26, 10.255 to 10.26, 15.26) are unchanged. Non-finite money now fails closed through the canonical engine in these helpers instead of flowing on as Infinity. Prospective only: amounts already stored under the EPSILON formula are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: the `= 2` default on the purchases-write rounder (normalizePositiveAmount relies on it without digits context — removing it is a separate fail-closed package); the legacy-named roundMoneyToTwoDecimals in financial-integrity (dead sale-payment validation path, pinned lenient contract) and quantity rounders (stock quantities, contracting quantities, reorder rates — quantities are not money); the reorder estimate helper (estimate-only, no digits context, and deriving digits there would add an FX-consistency failure to a read-only endpoint); and the single inline contracting labor-attendance total_payable rounding (no digits context in recordLaborAttendance — its own digits-plumbing package follows). This entry supersedes the EPSILON sentence in INV-POS-OFFER-DIGITS.
- INV-TABLE-BILL-DIGITS-AND-RATE-VALIDATION (31 October 2026): a dine-in table bill is never rounded at a hard-coded 2 decimals, and its configured rates never degrade silently. The held-draft branch of getActiveTableBill computes service charge, tax, and total through the pure computeTableBillTotals engine at the tenant's book digits (storefrontMoneyDigits from the settings the method already loads), and payActiveTableBill's bill-plus-tip total rounds through computeTableBillPaymentTotal at the digits of the bill's own currency — so a 3-decimal tenant's 10.125 bill keeps 1.215 / 1.701 / 13.041 instead of being cut to cents. The bill formulas are otherwise unchanged (service charge on subtotal minus discount, tax on the floored taxable base; subtotal and discount pass through raw), and the engine deliberately does not reuse computeInvoiceTotals, which rounds every result to 3 decimals internally. Both rates fail closed: an absent rate falls back (0 tax, 12 service percent) and a numeric string is accepted, but a garbled, negative, non-finite, or over-100 rate throws TABLE_BILL_RATE_INVALID (400) — the tax rate always, the service percent only while service charge is enabled, so a disabled charge still ignores a stale percent. Prospective only: bills already shown or paid at 2 decimals are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: the posted-sale branch, which echoes already-posted stored totals that must never be re-rounded; the frontend bill modal's display total (display mirror only — the backend payment total is authoritative); the delivery fee (a configured value, not computed); a negative held-draft discount (passed through exactly as before).
- INV-FX-CONVERSION-SINGLE-ENGINE (31 October 2026): FX conversion has one primitive — the pure convertFxAmount engine, which converts a foreign amount at a caller-supplied rate and rounds at the target currency's book digits through the canonical engine, failing closed on a non-finite amount or a non-finite/non-positive rate. The forex revaluation service is its first consumer: calculateForexDifference takes REQUIRED book digits and delegates to it, user-supplied foreign balances normalize at the foreign currency's digits, revalued amounts, differences, and totals round at the base currency's digits, and every hard-coded 0.01 gate (account inclusion, revaluation detection, gain/loss account creation, journal line inclusion) becomes one minor unit (10 ** -digits) — so a 3-decimal tenant's 100.13 USD at 48.5 revalues to 4856.305 instead of 4856.31, and a 0.005 difference is posted instead of skipped, while 2-decimal tenants compute exactly what they computed before. Storage is already safe: the revaluation run/line amount columns are NUMERIC(15,4), so 3-decimal values store exactly with no migration. Prospective only: revaluations already posted at 2 decimals are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: rate resolution (the closing rate stays a caller-supplied input validated by validateForexClosingRate; exchangeRateAsOf still has no production consumer, and unifying live/as-of resolution onto this engine is the next FX package); the bookRate fallback to the closing rate when no stored rate row exists (display and audit record only — the difference math uses GL balances, never bookRate); cross-currency (foreign-to-foreign) conversion, which convertCurrency still computes with its own display rounding; the maritime and import conversions, which migrate onto this engine one package at a time. Rate resolution is now closed by INV-FX-RATE-RESOLUTION-SINGLE-RESOLVER below; exchangeRateAsOf is reachable through the shared resolver, though no production caller passes an as-of moment yet.
- INV-FX-RATE-RESOLUTION-SINGLE-RESOLVER (31 October 2026): FX rate resolution has one resolver — resolveFxRate, which derives the base currency strictly through tenantBaseCurrency, returns 1 for the base currency (even with a supplied rate, and an omitted currency means the base currency), reads the append-only history through exchangeRateAsOf when an as-of moment is given (a supplied rate alongside a moment fails closed as a contradiction), accepts a validated supplied rate when no moment is given, and otherwise reads the live registered rate, failing closed when none exists. The maritime file-private resolver now delegates to it, so all eight maritime call sites resolve through the one path and the duplicated settings-parsed base derivation is gone; a tenant whose settings currency does not match its base row now fails closed at resolution instead of silently defaulting to EGP. The resolver returns the raw registered rate — rounding belongs to convertFxAmount downstream. Prospective only: resolutions already stored under the old resolver are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: pdcCurrencySnapshot, which keeps its PDC-only sanity/variance guards around the same live read and does not delegate here; the maritime container-deposit inline rate read and its 2-decimal conversion (the next maritime migration package); the revaluation bookRate read and closing-rate update (display/audit and write paths, not resolution); convertCurrency and the import rate derivation, which migrate onto the resolver one package at a time; no production caller passes an as-of moment yet, so historical-rate consumption remains a caller decision, not a default. The container-deposit item is now closed by INV-MARITIME-DEPOSIT-FX-CONVERSION below.
- INV-MARITIME-DEPOSIT-FX-CONVERSION (31 October 2026): a container-deposit 'open' journal converts through the shared FX stack — resolveFxRate on the same transaction for the rate, convertFxAmount at the base currency's book digits for the amount — so a 3-decimal tenant's 100.125 USD deposit at 48.5 posts 4856.063 instead of 4856.06, and 2-decimal tenants post exactly what they posted before. The inline rate query and its is_base shortcut are gone: a settings/rate mismatch now fails closed through tenantBaseCurrency, and a missing rate fails closed with the resolver's message. The refund and forfeit branches are deliberately unchanged: they reuse the opening journal's stored debit, keeping the historical rate the deposit was booked at, so no revaluation leaks into a refund. Prospective only: deposits already posted at 2 decimals are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: the deposit/expense conversions at the other maritime sites (3399/3496-style conversions), which migrate onto convertFxAmount one package at a time; the account resolution and period-open checks around the journal. The invoice/expense conversions are now closed by INV-MARITIME-JOB-JOURNAL-FX-CONVERSION below.
- INV-MARITIME-JOB-JOURNAL-FX-CONVERSION (31 October 2026): the maritime sales-invoice and expense-voucher journals convert through the shared FX stack — the rate already comes from the shared resolver, and the amount now converts through convertFxAmount at the base currency's book digits — so a 3-decimal tenant's 100.125 USD invoice or expense posts 4856.063 instead of 4856.06, and the job totals accumulated from those amounts (invoiced total, other-costs total, net profit) stay at book digits too, while 2-decimal tenants post exactly what they posted before. The hard-coded 2-decimal math is gone from both methods; no rate-resolution behavior changes, because both methods already resolved through the shared resolver. Prospective only: journals already posted at 2 decimals are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: the carrier-invoice journal conversion and the stored forex gain/loss computation (separate migration packages — the gain/loss is a rate-spread difference, not a single-rate conversion); the 4-decimal quote totals (presentation math, not postings); the customs duty percentage math (a percentage allocation, not FX); the account resolution, cost-center creation, and period-open checks around both journals. The carrier-invoice conversion is now closed by INV-MARITIME-CARRIER-INVOICE-FX-CONVERSION below.
- INV-MARITIME-CARRIER-INVOICE-FX-CONVERSION (31 October 2026): the maritime carrier-invoice journal converts through the shared FX stack — the rate already comes from the shared resolver, and the amount now converts through convertFxAmount at the base currency's book digits, reading the base currency on the same transaction — so a 3-decimal tenant's 100.125 USD carrier invoice posts 4856.063 instead of 4856.06, and the job's carrier-cost total and net profit accumulate the converted amount, while 2-decimal tenants post exactly what they posted before. The hard-coded 2-decimal math is gone from the method; no rate-resolution behavior changes, because the method already resolved through the shared resolver. Prospective only: carrier invoices already posted at 2 decimals are not rewritten. Deliberately unchanged: the stored forex gain/loss computation (a rate-spread difference, not a single-rate conversion — its own package); the supplier resolution, account resolution, cost-center handling, and period-open checks around the journal. The stored gain/loss is now closed by INV-MARITIME-FOREX-GAIN-LOSS-BOOK-DIGITS below.
- INV-MARITIME-FOREX-GAIN-LOSS-BOOK-DIGITS (31 October 2026): the stored maritime forex gain/loss rounds through the canonical roundMoney at the base currency's book digits, and the base currency comes strictly from tenantBaseCurrency instead of an inline is_base read with a silent EGP fallback — so a 3-decimal tenant's (50 - 48.5) * 100.125 USD spread stores 150.188 instead of 150.19, a negative spread stores signed at book digits, and a settings/rate mismatch fails closed with nothing stored, while 2-decimal tenants store exactly what they stored before. convertFxAmount is deliberately not used here: the spread can be negative or zero, and the engine fails closed on a non-positive rate; the stored value is a signed difference, rounded with the same canonical step. The multi-currency guard, the missing-quote-rate guard, the actual-rate validation, and the posted-invoices-only filter are unchanged. Prospective only: gain/loss values already stored at 2 decimals are not rewritten. Storage is safe without a migration: forex_gain_loss is NUMERIC(15,3).
- INV-LANDED-COST-EXCHANGE-RATE-REQUIRED (31 October 2026): calculateLandedCostBreakdown fails closed on a missing, zero, negative, or non-finite exchange rate instead of silently costing at rate 1; a tiny positive rate is used exactly as supplied, not clamped up to 0.0001 — so a shipment whose arrival rate was never set cannot produce plausible-looking landed costs that are wrong by the whole exchange rate. Valid rates pass through exactly, including sub-1 rates. The service caller already requires the arrival rate through requireImportNumber; this guard protects every direct caller of the pure engine. Deliberately unchanged: the 4-decimal/2-decimal rounding inside the breakdown, the allocation methods, the EGP field names and the landed_cost_egp storage (NUMERIC(10,2) — a digits/storage widening is its own migration decision, not part of this guard, now closed by INV-LANDED-COST-STORAGE-PRECISION below); the demurrage-risk estimate in the same file, whose optional rate default of 1 feeds a display estimate on the shipment-summary read path, not a costing conversion; the feasibility/quotation estimate defaults and the proforma rate carry-over (see INV-IMPORT-PROFORMA-EXCHANGE-RATE). Prospective only: landed costs already computed at the silent rate 1 are not recomputed.
- INV-LANDED-COST-STORAGE-PRECISION (31 October 2026): migration 2040000000271 widens import_shipment_items.allocated_overhead_egp and .landed_cost_egp from numeric(10, 2) to numeric(15, 4), following the 0263/0264/0265/0266 information_schema pattern (skip when already at scale 4, rollback never narrows) — so the breakdown engine's 4-decimal outputs and the late-expense 6-decimal increments store exactly instead of being cut to cents on write, and the inventory unit-cost reader no longer builds costs from truncated values. The (15, 4) target matches the sibling unit_landed_cost_egp column; widening only, existing values untouched. Deliberately unchanged: factory_unit_price_usd (a stored foreign unit price, its own decision); estimated_landed_cost_egp and unit_landed_cost_egp (already NUMERIC(15, 4)); the breakdown engine's rounding and allocation (unchanged by this package). Prospective only: rows already stored cut to cents are not recomputed — a recompute runs through the normal recalculation path, not this migration.
- INV-CONVERT-CURRENCY-TARGET-DIGITS (31 October 2026): the display conversion endpoint rounds its converted amount once, from the unrounded conversion, at the target currency's book digits through the canonical roundMoney — so 100.125 USD at 48.5 displays 4856.063 in KWD (3 digits) and 4856.06 in EGP (2 digits) instead of a fixed 4-decimal 4856.0625 in both. Deliberately unchanged: the live pair rate read, the fail-closed checks (base validation, registered currencies, positive finite rates), the base-leg conversion math, and the 6-decimal effective rate. This endpoint is display-only — nothing it returns is posted — so no journal, storage, or migration is touched. Deliberately unchanged: resolving either leg through resolveFxRate (a cross-currency display conversion is not a single-rate booking conversion; that migration is its own decision).
- INV-STOCK-COUNT-ACTUAL-DELTA (31 October 2026): stock-count posting uses the ACTUAL scoped delta -- scopeAfter minus scopeBefore from setScopedStockQty -- for the movement quantity and type, the movement cost, the import-lot deduction, the damage record, and the journal in postStockCount (which reads the delta from the movement row, falling back to the stored variance only for chunks posted before this fix). The counted quantity is the physical on-hand quantity, reservations included (decided 2026-10-31), so a location holding 100 with 10 reserved and a count of 95 posts a loss of 5 x WAC (Dr 5200 / Cr 1140), not a gain of 5 x WAC (Dr 1140 / Cr 7100) as the old available-basis variance did. The stored variance_qty stays the as-counted record; the stale check, chunking, trace reconciliation, and gain/loss/damage accounts are unchanged. Prospective only: journals already posted on the frozen variance are not rewritten -- recovery replays of old chunks read their old movement rows and reproduce the old totals. Deliberately unchanged: the available-basis expected_qty snapshot at session creation and the stale check that compares against it; the trace helper's comparison of the registered serial/batch ledger against that same available-basis snapshot (it fails closed with a ledger-mismatch error when reservations coincide with tracked items, rather than mis-posting); sessions whose counted quantity equals the available-basis expected quantity never reach the write branch, so a raw-vs-available gap on such an item persists until a recount; the zero-cost bypass in postStockCount (a zero-cost variance still posts nothing -- its own package); segregation of duties and the admin-password approval (their own package); the direct-adjustment and damaged-stock parallel writers.
- INV-MANUFACTURING-UNBUILD-JOURNAL (31 October 2026): createUnbuildOrder consumes the finished product at its LIVE weighted-average cost (products.cost_price, the same source completeWorkOrder uses) and recovers each BOM component at its live WAC, with money rounded once at the tenant's book digits; the BOM line's expected_cost is a planning estimate and is never used as a valuation. Both legs are stamped with unit_cost/total_cost on their stock movements, and postManufacturingUnbuildOrder posts a balanced journal in the SAME transaction, reading both legs from the stamped movements: Dr inventory 1140 for the recovered value, Cr inventory 1140 for the consumed finished-goods value, and the difference to the price-variance account 5190 (decided 2026-10-31) -- debit when the finished goods were worth more than the recovery, credit when the recovery is worth more, no 5190 line when the legs balance exactly. 5190 is resolved through resolveSystemAccountByCode, which auto-creates it as a known system account when absent (the same treatment GRN price variance gets). A zero-cost unbuild posts nothing and does not throw. No WAC recomputation runs on either leg: consuming and recovering at the current WAC leaves every WAC mathematically unchanged. The BOM waste percentage is deliberately NOT applied to recovery: the BOM quantity is the net content of the finished product, and the waste portion was consumed in production and cannot come back, so recovering the waste-inflated quantity would overstate stock. The product and the BOM must both be active (is_active), checked before any stock moves. Prospective only: unbuilds already completed keep their unjournalled stock moves and stale-cost order totals. Deliberately unchanged: the warehouse_id 0 sentinel when neither a warehouse nor a default location is set; the is_active state of component products (same as completeWorkOrder, which does not check it either); the work-order poster postManufacturingWorkOrder, including its overhead-as-residual derivation and its throw on genuinely zero-cost production (their own package); by-products, which do not exist on the unbuild path.
- INV-PHARMACY-BATCH-FROZEN (31 October 2026): batch identity is frozen in upsertBatch. A batch is created with a real expiry -- a calendar date (YYYY-MM-DD) or a real month (YYYY-MM, the form the acceptance suite already uses) -- and a status from the closed list active/expired/depleted; an already-expired batch cannot be created 'active'. After creation the expiry_date can never change (any differing value is rejected with PHARMACY_BATCH_EXPIRY_FROZEN, including moving it forward to re-admit an expired batch); repeating the stored value or omitting it stays allowed. Status changes are limited to the closed list, and 'expired' is terminal: a batch marked expired, or one whose expiry has passed, can never be flipped INTO 'active' through this endpoint (PHARMACY_BATCH_REACTIVATION_FORBIDDEN). The past-expiry boundary mirrors the FEFO sale gate exactly (month form against the current month, full date against today, both UTC), so a batch the gate would skip can never be re-activated as saleable here. Only explicit activations are gated: a notes-only update, or repeating the stored status, on a batch whose expiry passed with time is not blocked, and a row carrying a legacy status outside the list keeps it through a notes-only update. The pre-existing freeze on quantities, costs and links (PHARMACY_BATCH_FINANCIAL_EDIT_FORBIDDEN) is unchanged. Prospective only: batches already stored with malformed expiries or re-admitted statuses keep their stored values until a future correction flow; the gate below still protects sales. Deliberately unchanged: the FEFO sale gate itself (it already skips expired batches by string comparison); batch rows written by other paths (transfer/count trace copies, which copy the frozen source value; GRN/purchase intake, which creates stock through documents); the van field-sale FEFO bypass (its own package, sequenced after the batch-location decision); prescription/controlled-substance gates.
- INV-TRANSFER-RECEIPT-PINNED (31 October 2026): a transfer receipt must name an explicit received quantity for EVERY item -- an omitted item is rejected with TRANSFER_RECEIPT_INVALID instead of being booked as fully received, and a receipt with no items at all is rejected the same way. Any shortfall (dispatched minus received) requires a written variance reason of at least 8 characters (receipt-level, one reason per shipment), rejected with TRANSFER_VARIANCE_REASON_REQUIRED before any write; the reason is stored on each shortfall item's variance_reason, the first shrinkage journal id is stored on the header's transit_variance_journal_entry_id (both columns existed and were never written), and has_transit_variance reflects the shortfall. Shrinkage is valued at the cost frozen at dispatch -- the unit_cost stamped on the transfer_send movement at the tenant's book digits -- never at the receive-time cost_price; transfers sent before the stamp existed fall back to the current cost_price. The dispatch stamp, the receipt validation, and the reason persistence apply to the stock-transfer receive path only. Deliberately unchanged: no partial-receipt state (a transfer is received once, in full or with a reasoned shortfall); no segregation of duties on receipt; the final_issue mode (its journal and its Dr 6900 / Cr 1140 treatment are untouched -- only its movement cost now shares the same book-digits rounding); the category and internal transfer paths, which never call receiveStockTransfer; the trace reconciliation for serial/batch items; the in-transit GL treatment (transit goods stay inventory assets until receipt, a shortfall is the one leg that touches the global counter). Prospective only: transfers already received keep their old default-full quantities and unreasoned write-offs. Follow-up outside this package: the inventory receipt dialog sends every item today but has no reason field, so a shortfall receipt from the UI will be rejected until the dialog collects varianceReason.

---

## 1. كيف تقرأ هذا المشروع قبل أن تكتب فيه سطراً

| # | السؤال | الملف |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ما الحدود المعمارية (سحابة/ديسكتوب، عزل المستأجرين)؟ | `SAAS_ELECTRON_ISOLATION_CONSTITUTION.md` |
| 2 | ما قواعد الواجهة العربية والتصميم؟ | `GEMINI.md` |
| 3 | ما الميزات الموجودة فعلاً (لتجنب إعادة بناء منجز)؟ | `SYSTEM_CAPABILITIES.md` |
| 4 | **ما المحرمات والثوابت وأين دقَّقنا؟** | **هذا الملف** |
| 5 | قواعد العمل والتدقيق الحسابي | `AGENTS.md` (Rule 13) |

### الدرس الحاكم المستخلص من كل جولات التدقيق

> **النمط الأخطر في هذا المشروع ليس المنطق الخاطئ، بل المنطق الصحيح غير الموصول.**

تكرر هذا النمط في خمسة مواضع مستقلة على الأقل:

| الموضع | الصيغة كانت صحيحة | ولم تُنفَّذ لأن |
|---|---|---|
| استرداد الدفعة المقدمة (مقاولات) | نافذة `clamp` مكتوبة بدقة | الواجهة كانت ترسل النسبة دائماً فتقفز فوق الفرع |
| MATCH-1 (مشتريات) | المحرك يدعم `historicalInvoicedLines` | المستدعي لم يمرره إطلاقاً |
| MATCH-2 (مشتريات) | منطق فرق السعر سليم | أعمدة `po_id` لم تُنشأ في الهجرة |
| إعادة تقييم العملات | الحساب صحيح | الاستعلام يشير لأعمدة وجداول غير موجودة، مخفية بـ`as any` |
| `reconciliationDiscrepancy` | محسوب بدقة | لم يُفحص في أي شرط |

**لذلك:** لا تكتفِ بقراءة المنطق. **تتبَّع المسار من الواجهة إلى المحرك إلى قاعدة البيانات، وشغِّل الاختبار.** `tsc` يثبت اتساق الأنواع، لا أن المال يُخصم.

---

## 2. المصادر الوحيدة للحقيقة — لا تلتفّ عليها

### موافقات أوامر الشراء وقيود مرتجعات البيع (سبتمبر 2026)

- تأكيد أمر الشراء الذي يطابق قاعدة موافقة يجب أن يُنشئ `approval_requests` ويترك الأمر `pending_approval` داخل المعاملة نفسها. تعديل الحالة مباشرة من `PUT /api/purchase-orders/:id` أو تحويل أمر غير مؤكد إلى فاتورة يتجاوز الحوكمة. فشل إنشاء فاتورة الشراء النظامية يجب أن يوقف التحويل؛ يُمنع إنشاء رأس فاتورة احتياطي بلا بنود أو ترحيل.
- صاحب الطلب لا يعتمد طلبه. مدير المستوى المطلوب فقط يقرر، ويجب أن تُكتب حالة الطلب وسجل القرار وحالة أمر الشراء داخل معاملة واحدة؛ خطأ تحديث المستند يلغي القرار بالكامل.
- عكس تكلفة المخزون في مرتجع المبيعات يأخذ `sale_item_id` و`cost_price` للسطر الأصلي. إذا غاب معرف السطر وكان الصنف مكرراً بتكاليف مختلفة، يجب رفض الترحيل بدلاً من تخمين تكلفة أول سطر.
- حارس النشر يقبل تشغيل `workflow_run` الناجح فقط بعد `push` إلى `main` في المستودع نفسه. هوية خادم SSH تثبت بمفتاح موثوق في `ORACLE_SSH_KNOWN_HOSTS`، ولا يُعطَّل فحصها.

### إثبات جاهزية بيع SaaS (سبتمبر 2026)

- فحوص وجود ملفات الإصدار (`check:commercial-ready` و`check:go-live` و`qa:release`) فحوص بنيوية فقط؛ انتقال الوثائق إلى `docs/archive` لا يبرر فشلها، ونجاحها لا يساوي توقيع UAT أو تحقق بريد SMTP أو تشغيل الإنتاج.
- اختبارات HTTP لدورة المستأجر يجب أن تنشئ مستأجر اختبار منفصلاً وتحذفه بعد النجاح أو الفشل. مسار CI يغطي التسجيل العام ومنع التكرار، وإنشاء التجربة إدارياً، وعزل لوحة المنصة، والتعليق والتفعيل والانتهاء والتجديد من غير المرور ببوابات الدفع.
- تفعيل وحدة ZATCA في بيئة الإنتاج لا يتم إلا بعد استلام `binarySecurityToken` و`secret` صالحين من الهيئة؛ أي رد فاشل أو ناقص يبقي الوحدة غير مفعلة. المحاكاة مسموحة لوحدات `sandbox` و`simulation` فقط.
- نسخ قاعدة بيانات SaaS التي تغادر السيرفر يجب أن تكون مشفرة دائمًا. غياب مفتاح التشفير يوقف المهمة، وفشل تجميع حزمة العملاء يستخدم dump كاملًا مشفرًا كبديل ولا يرفع نسخة خام.

هذه دوال/وحدات **إلزامية**. أي كتابة مباشرة بديلة عنها أنتجت خطأ حقيقياً في الماضي.

### 2.1 حركة المخزون → `applyStockDelta`

**الملف:** `backend/src/common/utils/location-stock-ledger.ts`

```ts
await applyStockDelta(trx, { productId, delta, branchId, locationId, tenantId, accountId, ... });
```

**محظور:** كتابة `product_location_stock` أو `products.stock_qty` بـ`UPDATE`/`INSERT` مباشر.

**لماذا:** الالتفاف عليها سبَّب ثلاث فئات من الأعطال فعلياً:
1. **انحراف الرصيد العام** — `van-sales` كان يكتب المواقع فقط ولا يمس `products.stock_qty` ولا `stock_movements`. الرصيد العام كان ينفصل عن مجموع المواقع في كل رحلة.
2. **استعصاء ABBA** — الترتيب القانوني هو `products` ثم `product_location_stock`. القفل بالعكس يُسبب Deadlock مع مسار متزامن.
3. **تجاوز `ensureNonNegativeStock`** ومنطق تفريغ الرصيد غير المخصص.

**ترتيب الأقفال القانوني (لا يُخالَف):**
```
products (FOR UPDATE)  →  product_location_stock (FOR UPDATE)
```
وفي مسار البيع يُستدعى `lockProductRow()` قبل حلقة المواقع لضمان هذا الترتيب.
**وبنود الفاتورة تُرتَّب تصاعدياً بـ`productId`** قبل حلقة المخزون — ترتيب العميل عشوائي ويُسبب Deadlock.

### 2.2 القيود المحاسبية → `insertPostedJournal`

**الملف:** `backend/src/modules/accounting/accounting-posting.service.ts`

يوفّر ثلاثة أشياء لا يوفرها الإدراج اليدوي:
- **حارس التوازن المركزي** (`:421-425`) يرفض أي قيد مدين ≠ دائن.
- **فحص قفل الفترة** بثلاث طبقات (`lock_date_all` / `lock_date_non_adviser` / `lock_date_tax`).
- **ترقيم آمن:** رقم مؤقت ثم إعادة تسمية بالمعرّف.

**مدخل الوحدات التي تبني سطورها بنفسها:** `postDomainJournal(queryable, params)` — عام، يفوّض إلى `insertPostedJournal`. وُجد لأن "سطوري خاصة" ليس سبباً لكتابة `journal_entries` باليد: المستدعي يملك **أي** حسابات ومبالغ تُكوّن القيد، ولا يملك هل يتوازن، وهل تقبله الفترة، وبأي رقم يُرقَّم.

**محظور:** `COUNT(*) + 1` لتوليد `entry_no`. سبَّب تضارب أرقام قيود تحت التزامن في مسارين (GRN والتكاليف الإضافية).

**محظور:** `if (account) { ...post... }` — التخطي الصامت للقيد. سبَّب اختلال ميزانية بكامل قيمة إذن الاستلام بلا خطأ ولا أثر. استخدم `resolveAccountByCode` الذي **يُنشئ الحساب الناقص** أو يرمي استثناءً.

**محظور:** عكس شيك مرتد عند الإلغاء أو الرد (`postPdcChequeCancelOrReturn` on `status === 'bounced'`) — الارتداد قام بالفعل بإعادة المديونية لحساب العميل/المورد في الأستاذ العام ودفتر الأستاذ المساعد، وتكرار القيد عند رد الشيك المرتد يضاعف المديونية.

**إلزامية الربط التبادلي للقيود العكسية:** كل قيد عكسي أو إلغاء أو استعادة لشيك أو فاتورة يجب أن يربط `reversal_of_entry_id` بالقيد الأصلي، ويُحدّث `reversed_by_entry_id` على القيد الأصلي ليصبح القيد الأصلي معكوماً ومعلوماً من عكسه. الفهرس الفريد للشيكات والخصم والإضافة في هجرة `247` يستثني القيود المعكوسة (`WHERE reversed_by_entry_id IS NULL`) ليسمح بإعادة إيداع الشيكات المستعادة بأمان.

**إلزامية مطابقة الأستاذ المساعد (Sub-Ledger Sync):** كافة حركات الشيكات (استلام، إصدار، ارتداد، تظهير، رد، واستعادة) وحركات الأرصدة الافتتاحية للموردين والعملاء وحركات درج النقدية اليدوية (`cash_in`, `cash_out`) وحركات فوائد التقسيط يجب أن تسجل قيداً مزدوجاً متوازناً في الأستاذ العام وتحدث رصيد الشريك وجدول الأستاذ المساعد (`customer_ledger` / `supplier_ledger`) في نفس المعاملة لمنع أي تباين بين ميزان المراجعة وتقارير أعمار الديون.

**دستور إقرار القيمة المضافة لأسعار شاملة الضريبة:** عند حساب الوعاء الخاضع للضريبة للفواتير شاملة الضريبة (`prices_include_tax = true`)، الوعاء يساوي `(subtotal - discount) - tax_amount` لمنع تضخيم الوعاء الضريبي المرفوع لمصلحة الضرائب.

**إلزامية إقفال حسابات المقاصة وحسابات الدخل الأخرى (Contra Accounts & Other Income/Expenses):** دالة تصنيف الأرباح والخسائر `fiscalProfitAndLossCategory` وملخص المركز المالي تشمل حسابات `contra_revenue` (مردودات وخصومات المبيعات `4300` و `4400`) وحسابات `contra_expense` لتصفيرها بالكامل وإقفال صافي أثرها في حساب الأرباح المحتجزة `3200`. استبعادها كان يترك رصيدها مفتوحاً في العام المالي الجديد ويشوه الأرباح المبقاة.

**مقارنة التواريخ:** دائماً بنص `YYYY-MM-DD`، لا بكائنات `Date`. المقارنة بـ`Date` جعلت إذن استلام الساعة 09:00 في يوم القفل يمر في مسار ويُحجب في آخر.
